\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\sim(L\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+\psi^{2}.

To state the resulting weighted versions of estimate (
3.3
) let us introduce some notation.
For
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
we define

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(r^{-1}L(r\psi))^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}(L\psi)^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2},

(3.46)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)+\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-2}\psi^{2},

(3.47)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}\left((r^{-1}L(r\psi))^{2}+(L\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)+r^{p-3}\psi^{2}.

(3.48)

We also define versions of (
3.48
) where
the first term is replaced by
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)
or
{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)
.
We will call these
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)
and
{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau)
.
Note that these latter two expressions do not control a zero’th order term in the region
r<R
.

Note the following properties. For
2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta
,
we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\mathcal{E}},\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\mathcal{F}}{\rm\ \ for\ }p\geq p^{\prime}\geq\delta{\rm\ or\ }p^{\prime}=0,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}{\rm\ for\ }p\geq 1+\delta,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}{\rm\ for\ }p\geq 1.

(3.49)

Let us also define the fluxes

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau):=\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma(\tau)}^{\mu},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau):=\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mu},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau):=\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}.

(3.50)

We note that by (
3.45
), it follows that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)

(3.51)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau).

(3.52)

We may now state the main result of this section:

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
satisfy the assumptions of Section 2 and Section
3.4.4
, and
let
(V,w,q,\varpi)
be as in Section
3.4.4
.
(In particular, the estimates (
3.3
) and (
3.33
) are assumed to hold.)
Assume, for all
0<\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, the existence of
a quadruple
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}_{\rm far})
as above
satisfying (
3.39
) and the far away coercivity properties (
3.40
) and (
3.41
).

Then for all
0<\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, the following estimate holds for all

\tau_{0}\leq\tau
:

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

(3.53)

\displaystyle\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau_{0})+A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\left|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}[\psi]F\right|+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}F^{2}

as well as the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

(3.54)

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}(\tau_{0})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\left(|r^{p}r^{-1}L(r\psi)|+|\tilde{V}_{p}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\psi|+|\tilde{w}_{p}\psi|\right)|F|+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}F^{2},

where
\tilde{V}_{p}
is a fixed vector field and
\tilde{w}_{p}
a fixed function satisfying

|g(\tilde{V}_{p},L)|\lesssim 1,\qquad|g(\tilde{V}_{p},\underline{L})|\lesssim 1,\qquad\sum|g(\tilde{V}_{p},\Omega_{i})|^{2}\lesssim 1,\qquad|\tilde{w}_{p}|\lesssim r^{-1}.

(3.55)

Remark 3.5.2
.

In the case where we replace the middle term of (
3.3
)
with (
3.10
), we
should add the first term of (
3.10
) to the right hand side of (
3.54
).

Proof.

The estimate (
3.53
) follows immediately from the energy identity (
3.15
)
corresponding
to the current
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}
, in view of the properties assumed.

Note that in cases (i) or (ii), estimate (
3.53
) already implies (
3.54
),
in view also of (
3.39
). In general, to obtain (
3.54
), we add a large multiple
of estimate (
3.3
) to (
3.53
). This allows us to absorb the term
multiplying
A
on the right hand side of (
3.53
)
in view of the trivial relation

\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}.

∎

For consistency, we will in what follows often denote the quadruple
(V,w,q,\varpi)
of Section
3.4.4

as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{V}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{w}
,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{q}
,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\varpi}
, and the currents as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{J}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{K}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{H}
.

Finally, in view of Proposition
3.4.2
, given
\varsigma\geq 1
, we may define versions of the above currents where
V_{\varsigma}
,
w_{\varsigma}
,

q_{\varsigma}
,
\varpi_{\varsigma}

replace
V
,
w
,
q
,
\varpi
, respectively, in definition (
3.42
).
We will denote these currents as

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\varsigma},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\varsigma},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma}.

All boundedness and coercivity inequalities will continue to hold with constants independent of
\varsigma
in the region
r\geq r_{\rm Killing}
,
while in the region
r<r_{\rm Killing}
, the resulting
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\varsigma}
will satisfy the enhanced positivity property (
3.35
), at the expense
of lower order terms.
(In the region
r<r_{\rm Killing}
, however, we emphasise the dependence of the coercivity constants on
\varsigma
.)

3.6
Higher order estimates

As is well known, in applications to nonlinear problems, one must be able to prove
higher order estimates in order for the estimates to close.

3.6.1
The commutation vector fields
\mathfrak{D}
and the auxiliary
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}

Let
\zeta(r)
denote a smooth cutoff function
such that
\zeta=1
for
r\geq 3R/4
and
\zeta=0
for
r\leq R/2
. If
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
, let
\hat{\zeta}=0
.
Otherwise, let
\hat{\zeta}(r)
denote a smooth cutoff function
such that
\hat{\zeta}=1
for
r\leq r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/4
and
\hat{\zeta}=0
for
r\geq r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/2
.
If
\Omega_{1}
is Killing, we define
\upsilon=1
, otherwise we set
\upsilon=0
.
(More generally, we may take
\upsilon=0
if
T
coincides with the Killing generator and is timelike in
r>r_{\rm Killing}
.)

We define

\mathfrak{D}_{1}=T,\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{2}=\upsilon\Omega_{1},\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{3}=Y,\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{4}=\zeta L,\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{5}=\zeta\underline{L},\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{5+i}=(\zeta+\hat{\zeta})\Omega_{i},\,i=1,\ldots,3.

(3.56)

We will denote
{\bf k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4},k_{5},k_{6},k_{7},k_{8})
,
|{\bf k}|=\sum_{i=1}^{8}k_{i}

and
by
\mathfrak{D}^{{\bf k}}\psi
the expression

\mathfrak{D}^{{\bf k}}\psi=\mathfrak{D}_{1}^{k_{1}}\mathfrak{D}_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{D}_{8}^{k_{8}}\psi.

(3.57)

Note that the vector fields (
3.56
)
span the tangent space for
r\geq 3R/4
and, if
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
, in
r\leq r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/4
,
but not so in general for
r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/4\leq r\leq 3R/4
. It is also useful to have a collection of operators
that do. We thus define
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}

to denote commutation strings of operators from the collection

\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{1}=L,\qquad\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{2}=\underline{L},\qquad\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{2+i}=\Omega_{i},\qquad i=1,\ldots,6,

(3.58)

i.e. we define

\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\bf k}}\psi=\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{1}^{k_{1}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}_{8}^{k_{8}}\psi.

(3.59)

(Recall that the additional
\Omega_{i}
,
i=4,5,6
were introduced in the case
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
to ensure the existence of a convenient
globally defined
spanning set of vectors. In the case
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
, we may understand the above formula with
\Omega_{4}=\Omega_{5}=\Omega_{6}=0
.)

3.6.2
Assumption on commutation errors in
r\geq R_{k}

As is to be expected, in order to obtain higher order estimates on (
3.3
),
we will need to strengthen our asymptotic flatness assumption
to a higher order statement on
g_{0}
.
Again, instead of formulating sufficient conditions in terms of the decay properties of
g_{0}
for large
r
,
it will be convenient here to directly assume exactly the statement we shall need,
in terms of decay properties of the coefficients appearing in

[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi
.

Our assumption is thus simply the following: For all
k\geq 1
, there exists an
R_{k}
such that
the following pointwise bound for
p=0
and for
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
holds in
r\geq R_{k}

\left|\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi][\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\right|\leq\frac{1}{12}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]

(3.60)

for all smooth functions
\psi
.

Again, we emphasise that by our conventions of Section
3.1
,
the constant
C
on the right hand side of (
3.60
) in general
depends on
k
. (In actuality, we need only assume (
3.60
) for all
k\leq k_{\rm asympt}
for some sufficiently large

k_{\rm asympt}
, but the statement
of our theorem will then be restricted to such
k
.)

Assumption (
3.60
) is easily seen to be satisfied in all examples of Section
2.7
.
We note that the
k
dependence of
R_{k}
is in general necessary, even
for Minkowski space, as we must take
R_{k}\to\infty
as
k\to\infty
.

3.6.3
The red-shift commutation

We recall the following

Proposition 3.6.1

(
[
DR13
]
)
.

Let
Y
be the vector field of Section
2.6.4
,
and let
\mathcal{H}^{+}
be a Killing horizon with positive surface gravity as assumed in
Section
2.4
,
such that the generator
Z
lies in the span of
T
and
\Omega_{1}
. Then, along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we have

[Y^{k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi=\kappa_{k}(\vartheta,\varphi)(Y^{k+1}\psi)+Y^{k}\Box_{g_{0}}\psi+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1,k_{3}<k+1}\alpha_{\bf k}(\vartheta,\phi)({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi),

for a
\kappa_{k}\geq c>0
.

We have

Corollary 3.6.2
.

Let
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma}
be as defined in Section
3.5
. Then there exist constants
c>0
,
C>0
(independent of
\varsigma
)
such that along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}[Y^{k}\psi][Y^{k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\geq c(Y^{k+1}\psi)^{2}-C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1,k_{3}\neq k+1}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}.

(3.61)

Again, we emphasise that by our conventions of Section
3.1
,
the constants
c
and
C
on the right hand side of (
3.61
) in general
depend on
k
.

3.6.4
Divergence identity for the higher order master currents

We define the following positive signature function for
1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k

\displaystyle\sigma({\bf k},k)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{12}(k){\rm\ if\ }k_{1}+k_{2}=|{\bf k}|

(3.62)

\displaystyle=1{\rm\ otherwise}

where
\sigma_{12}
will be chosen later, such that
moreover
\sigma_{12}\geq 1
.

We define an additional signature function
\varsigma(k)
, for
k\geq 1
also to be determined later, which will be used
to select the parameter of Proposition
3.4.2
to be used in the currents for higher order estimates.

Let us finally fix a positive function

\varkappa({\bf k},k)=\varkappa(|{\bf k}|,k)=(\varkappa_{0}(k))^{1-|{\bf k}|}

(3.63)

for a

\varkappa_{0}(k)\geq 1
to be determined later.

Given the above commutation vector fields (
3.56
), and the notation (
3.57
),
we may now define currents

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=\varkappa(0,k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}[\psi]+\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa({\bf k},k)\sigma({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\varsigma(k)}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi],

(3.64)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=\varkappa(0,k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[\psi]+\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa({\bf k},k)\sigma({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\varsigma(k)}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi],

and given a collection
G_{k}=\{G_{\bf k}\}_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}
of scalar functions, the current

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot G_{k}:=\varkappa_{0}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}[\psi]G_{0}+\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa({\bf k},k)\sigma({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma(k)}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]G_{\bf k},

(3.65)

where
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}
, as well as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\varsigma}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\varsigma}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma}
,
are as defined in Section
3.5
.

If
\psi
satisfies the inhomogeneous equation (
3.3
),
then
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
satisfies the equation

\Box_{g_{0}}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)=[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi+\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F

and the currents satisfy

\nabla^{\mu}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}{}_{\mu}[\psi]=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\},

(3.66)

which can again be integrated in region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)
to yield

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma(\tau_{1},v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[\psi]\cdot n+\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[\psi]\cdot n+\int_{\underline{C}_{v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[\psi]\cdot n+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma(\tau_{0},v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[\psi]\cdot n-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\}.

(3.67)

Note that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}=0

(3.68)

in
r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/2\leq r\leq R/2
.

Finally, if

\varkappa_{0}(k)\gg\varsigma(k)

(3.69)

is sufficiently large, then we notice that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]\geq 0
even for
r\leq r_{\rm Killing}
,
and in fact in
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}(\varsigma)
, we have

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]\geq c\varkappa_{0}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[\psi]

(3.70)

\displaystyle\qquad+c\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa({\bf k},k)\varsigma\lambda_{\varsigma}(r)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{3}(\Omega_{i}\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+(T\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|r_{\rm Killing}-r|(Y\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\right)+\varkappa({\bf k},k)\lambda_{\varsigma}(r)(Y\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

where we have absorbed the lower order term in (
3.35
) with the wrong sign by the largeness of
\varkappa_{0}(k)=\varkappa({|\bf k}|,k)^{-1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|-1,k)
and the positivity of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[\psi]
, and we have dropped the factor
\sigma({\bf k},k)
using that
\sigma\geq 1
.
We will always assume (
3.69
) in what follows so that (
3.70
) holds.

Defining

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma(\tau)}^{\mu},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau):=\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mu},

(3.71)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu},

note that by construction

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)\geq 0,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)\geq 0,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)\geq 0,

and thus, from (
3.67
)
we have in particular

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]

\displaystyle\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\geq R/2\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\}|,

(3.72)

provided the terms on the right hand side of the above inequalities are suitably integrable.

3.6.5
Elliptic estimates for
\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=F

Since in the region
r\leq 3R/4
our commutation operators
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}
do not necessarily span the tangent space, we will need
to also invoke elliptic estimates.
These will allow one to estimate, for solutions of
\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=F
,
all highest order derivatives
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi
from only derivatives
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
with respect to
our commutation operators together with appropriate terms involving
F
.
These estimates require integration over hypersurfaces
\Sigma(\tau)
or spacetime regions
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
,
and rely on the fact
that the commutation operators
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
span an appropriate timelike direction.

For estimates at order
k
, we will in fact more generally need spacetime elliptic estimates in
r\leq 9R_{k}/8
, despite
the fact that the
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}
span the tangent space as long as
r\geq 3R/4
,
in order to absorb commutation error
terms where the formula (
3.60
) does not yet apply.

We have the following proposition:

Proposition 3.6.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
satisfy the assumptions of Section
2
.
Let
\psi
be a solution of the inhomogeneous equation (
4.4
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and let
\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}
.
Then for all
k\geq 1
and for all
r_{\rm Killing}<r^{\prime}_{-}<r^{\prime}<r^{\prime\prime}<r^{\prime\prime}_{+}\leq R
,

\int_{\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r^{\prime}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf{{k}}}\psi)^{2}\\
\lesssim\int_{\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r^{\prime}_{-}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}_{+}\}}\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1,k_{1}+k_{2}\geq|{\bf k}|-1}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq 1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf{k}}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2},

(3.73)

where here
\lesssim
depends on the choice of
r^{\prime}_{-}<r^{\prime}<r^{\prime\prime}<r_{+}^{\prime\prime}
.
We also have the estimate, for all
r^{\prime}\leq r_{1}
,

\int_{\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r\geq r^{\prime}\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf{{k}}}\psi)^{2}\\
\lesssim\int_{\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r\geq r^{\prime}\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}.

(3.74)

Note that the analogous statements to (
3.73
), (
3.74
)
with integration on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r^{\prime}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}\}
, etc.,
follows immediately in view of the coarea formula.

In fact, even without assuming
r^{\prime\prime}_{+}\leq R
, we still have the spacetime elliptic estimate:

\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r^{\prime}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf{{k}}}\psi)^{2}\\
\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r^{\prime}_{-}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}_{+}\}}\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1,k_{1}+k_{2}\geq|{\bf k}|-1}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq 1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf{k}}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2},

(3.75)

where again
\lesssim
depends on the choice of
r^{\prime}_{-}<r^{\prime}<r^{\prime\prime}<r_{+}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Estimate (
3.73
) is a standard elliptic estimate, using the fact that the span of

T,\Omega_{1}
always contains a timelike direction
in the region
r\geq r^{\prime}_{-}>r_{\rm Killing}
. Estimate (
3.74
) then follows from the previous in view
of the fact that the
\mathfrak{D}^{k}

span the tangent space for
r\leq r_{1}
and
r\geq R/2
.

As we have remarked, in
the case
r^{\prime\prime}_{+}\leq R
, estimate (
3.75
) follows from (
3.73
) by the coarea formula.
It can be obtained more generally, even if
R\leq r^{\prime\prime}
or
R\leq r^{\prime\prime}_{+}
by a suitable integration by parts argument. We sketch this here for
the case
k=1
. Let us consider the most difficult case where
R<r^{\prime\prime}<r^{\prime\prime}_{+}
. One first does the usual elliptic estimates on
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}
, then
integrating over
\tau
,
to estimate the left hand side of (
3.75
) integrated over
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R\}
from the right hand
side, but with an additional boundary term on
r=R
. Squaring the inhomogeneous wave equation in
r\geq R

and integrating by parts along the null cone
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}
, one may again bound the left hand side of (
3.75
), integrated over

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{R\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}\}
, from the right hand side and an additional boundary term on
r=R
. These boundary
terms can be arranged to cancel modulo a term of the form
\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r=R\}}T\psi\not{\Delta}\psi
,
where
\not{\Delta}
denotes the Laplacian on
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r=R\}
, and this term can in turn be related
to a bulk integral which can again be controlled by the right hand side of (
3.75
).
∎

3.6.6
Global control of the commutation errors

We may now give the final statement allowing for the global
control of the commutation error terms in the identity (
3.66
).

Proposition 3.6.4
.

Under the assumptions of Section
3.6.2
,
we may choose our weight functions
\sigma_{12}(k)
in the definition (
3.62
),
\varkappa_{0}(k)

in the definition (
3.63
) and
\varsigma(k)
,
so that
the following holds:

For all
k\geq 1
, there exists
r_{\rm Killing}<r_{1}(k)\leq r_{1}
, such that,
for all

\psi
satisfying the inhomogeneous equation (
4.4
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi],\qquad r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}(k)

(3.76)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{1}(k)\leq r_{2}\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{\rm Killing}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\frac{1}{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}|\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F|^{2},

(3.77)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad r_{1}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{2}-r_{1})\leq r\leq R/2

(3.78)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{R/2\leq r\leq R_{k}\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{R/4\leq r\leq 9R_{k}/8\}}\frac{1}{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}|\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F|^{2},

(3.79)

\displaystyle|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{10}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi],\qquad r\geq R_{k}.

(3.80)

Proof.

We first prove (
3.80
). Here we will use (
3.60
).
We have that the left hand side of (
3.80
) is bounded in
r\geq R_{k}
by

\displaystyle\left|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi][\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\sigma({\bf k},k)\varkappa({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|+1,k)\sigma({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\sigma({\bf k},k)\varkappa({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\varkappa(|{\bf k}|+1,k)\varkappa^{-1}(|{\bf k}|,k))\varkappa(|{\bf k}|,k)\sigma({\bf k},k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+\frac{1}{100}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]\leq\frac{1}{10}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]

provided that
\varkappa({\bf k},k)
is defined so that

\varkappa_{0}^{-1}(k)\ll 1.

(3.81)

To prove (
3.76
), we note that in the region
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}
, we may estimate:

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}=-\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k,k_{3}<k}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|,k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma(k)}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]\,[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi-\varkappa(k,k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma(k)}[Y^{k}\psi][Y^{k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi

(3.82)

\displaystyle\leq C\varsigma(k)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|,k)\left((T{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}(\Omega_{i}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\varsigma(k)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|-1,k)({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}

(3.83)

\displaystyle\qquad-c\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)\varkappa(k,k)(Y^{k+1}\psi)^{2}+C\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)|r-r_{\rm Killing}|\varkappa(k,k)\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}.

Here, we have applied (
3.61
) from
Corollary
3.6.2
, and
the bounds on
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\varsigma(k)}
following from (
3.36
) of Proposition
3.4.2
.
Note the dependence on
\varsigma(k)
through

\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)
,
and note that the terms in
the commutation identity not present on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
appear with an extra
(r-r_{\rm Killing})
factor.
(We emphasise again the conventions from Section
3.1
that constants
C
,
c
, also depends on
k
!)

We now have that given
\varsigma=\varsigma(k)
, from (
3.70
), it follows that
in the region
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}(\varsigma)
,
provided that (
3.69
) holds,
we have

\displaystyle\varsigma(k)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|,k)\left((T{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}(\Omega_{i}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\right)\leq C\varsigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi].

\displaystyle\varsigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k)\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|-1)({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\leq C\varsigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi].

Finally, again provided (
3.69
) holds, and provided we further restrict
r_{1}(\varsigma)
such that
0<r_{1}(\varsigma)-r_{\rm Killing}\leq\varsigma^{-1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varsigma(k)}(r)|r-r_{\rm Killing}|\varkappa(k,k)\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\leq C\varsigma^{-1}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi].

It follows that, for
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}(\varsigma)
, we have

-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}\leq C\varsigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi].

(3.84)

Now we fix
\varsigma=\varsigma(k)
to be
sufficiently large such that
C\varsigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k)\leq\frac{1}{3}
.
This yields (
3.76
).

Since
\varsigma(k)
is now fixed, as well as
r_{1}(k):=r_{1}(\varsigma(k))
,
according to our conventions, the dependence on these parameters may now be absorbed into the constants
C
,
c
, etc.

To prove (
3.77
),
we estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{1}(k)\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}

\displaystyle|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{1}(k)\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k+1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|-1,k)(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{\rm Killing}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq|{\bf k}|\leq k+1,k_{1}+k_{2}\geq|{\bf k}|-1}\varkappa(|{\bf k}|-1,k)({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq 1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r_{\rm Killing}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}}\sigma_{12}^{-1}(k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+C\varkappa^{-1}(0,k)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}.

(We emphasise again the conventions from Section
3.1
that constants
C
also depends on
k
.)
We have used the spacetime version of estimate (
3.73
) of Proposition
3.6.3
in the above (i.e. (
3.75
)), with

r^{\prime}_{-}:=r_{\rm Killing}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{1}(k)-r_{\rm Killing})
,
r^{\prime\prime}_{+}:=r_{1}+3(r_{2}-r_{1})/4
,
r^{\prime}=r_{1}(k)
,
r^{\prime\prime}=r_{1}+(r_{2}-r_{1})/2
, where
we also use that the integrand on the left hand side is nonzero only in
r^{\prime}\leq r\leq r^{\prime\prime}
.

Requiring now
\sigma_{12}(k)\gg 1
to be sufficiently large and
\varkappa(0,k)=\varkappa_{0}(k)\gg 1
to be sufficiently large, we obtain (
3.77
).

The proof of (
3.79
) is analogous to (
3.77
),
and also constrains
\sigma_{12}(k)
,
\varkappa_{0}(k)
to be sufficiently large.
This finally fixes
\sigma_{12}(k)
and
\varkappa_{0}(k)
.
Note here we use (
3.75
)
with
r^{\prime\prime}=R_{k}
and
r^{\prime\prime}_{+}=9R_{k}/8
and in general we may have
9R_{k}/8\geq R
.

Identity (
3.78
) follows immediately from the definition of the commutation
vector fields
\mathfrak{D}
.
∎

In what follows, we shall consider
\varsigma
,
\sigma
and
\varkappa
fixed so as to satisfy the above Proposition.
Thus, from now on, dependence of constants on
\varsigma
,
\sigma
and
\varkappa
will be absorbed in the
C
and

\lesssim
notation.
We emphasise again that, according to our conventions from Section
3.1
,
all our constants
c
,
C
will in general depend on
k
.

We have the following immediate corollary of Proposition
3.6.4
:

Corollary 3.6.5
.

Let
k\geq 1
and let

\psi
be a solution of the inhomogeneous equation (
4.4
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
Then
we have the global (one-sided) bound:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}

\displaystyle-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\geq r_{2}\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

(3.85)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}\cap\{r\geq R/4\}}\frac{1}{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}|\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F|^{2}.

Proof.

Note that the presence of
\frac{1}{2}
instead of
\frac{1}{3}
is due to the fact that both estimates (
3.79
)
and (
3.80
) borrow from the bulk of the region
r\geq R_{k}
. Note that restricted to the region
r\geq R
, if desired, we may of course
replace the
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}
commutation on the last term on the right hand side by
\mathfrak{D}
.
∎

3.6.7
The higher order energy notation

We are now ready to derive the higher order weighted estimates which will allow
us in particular to address nonlinear problems.
We will turn to the estimates themselves in Section
3.6.8
.
In the meantime, let us introduce some notation below.

We proceed to summarise the definitions:
Given
k\geq 1
,
1\leq\tau_{0}\leq\tau
,
v
,
and a spacetime function
\psi
, we define the following energies.

In general, our fundamental energies without degeneration functions
\chi
or
\rho

will be defined with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}
as commutation vector fields, i.e. they will contain all derivatives at the relevant
order.
Specifically, we define first the unweighted energies:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}(\underline{L}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.86)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau)}(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(\underline{L}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.87)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}(\underline{L}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+(\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.88)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}r^{-1-\delta}\left((L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}\right)+r^{-3-\delta}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}.

(3.89)

We now define (in analogy with (
3.46
)–(
3.48
)) the higher order
p
-weighted energies for
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p}(r^{-1}L(r\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.90)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v)+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}r^{p}|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.91)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}r^{p-1}\left((r^{-1}L(r\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}\right)+r^{p-3}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}.

(3.92)

Note the important relation:

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k$}}}}{\mathcal{E}}},\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}{\rm\ \ for\ }p\geq p^{\prime}\geq\delta{\rm\ or\ }p^{\prime}=0,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}{\rm\ for\ }p\geq 1+\delta,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}{\rm\ for\ }p\geq 1,

(3.93)

representing the higher order analogue of (
3.49
)

In contrast, we define the higher order energies carrying the degenerations
functions
\chi
,
\rho
and
\tilde{\rho}
in terms of the
\mathfrak{D}
commutators:

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}r^{-1-\delta}\chi(r)\left((L\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi|^{2}\right),

(3.94)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}r^{-1-\delta}\rho(r)\left((L\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi|^{2}\right),

(3.95)

\displaystyle{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}\tilde{\rho}(r)r^{-3-\delta}(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}

(3.96)

and then

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:={}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}\left((r^{-1}L(r\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}\right)+r^{p-3}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2},

(3.97)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle={}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{p-1}\left((r^{-1}L(r\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+(L\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}\right)+r^{p-3}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}.

(3.98)

(Note that in the integrals over
r\geq R
, it would not matter whether we use
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}
or
\mathfrak{D}
as these would here define
directly comparable energies.)

In analogy with (
3.30
)
we define

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}\xi(r)(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}.

(3.99)

We note the fundamental relation

\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}

(3.100)

which follows from our constraints on the support of
\xi
and the degeneration of
\chi
.

Proposition 3.6.6
.

For a general smooth function
\psi
, we have the following relations between energies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau),

(3.101)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

(3.102)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\tilde{A}\xi(r)(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},\qquad

\displaystyle 2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta

(3.103)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[\psi]+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\tilde{A}\xi(r)(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},\qquad

\displaystyle p=0.

For
\psi
a solution of the inhomogeneous equation
\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=F
, we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}.

(3.104)

If
\chi=1
and
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically (as in case (i)),
then

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2},\quad 2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta

(3.105)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2},

(3.106)

and if
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically (i.e. as in cases (i) and (ii)), then

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2},\quad 2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta

(3.107)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}.

(3.108)

Proof.

The relations (
3.101
)–(
3.103
)
follow immediately from our coercivity and boundedness assumptions on the currents
while the inequalities (
3.104
)–(
3.108
) follow easily from the elliptic
estimate (
3.74
)
of Proposition
3.6.3
.
∎

We note the following immediate corollary of the above proposition:

Corollary 3.6.7
.

Let
\psi
be a solution of
\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=0
in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
Then calligraphic and fraktur energies on
\Sigma(\tau)

are equivalent

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau).

If
\chi=1
and
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically (as in case (i)), then

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau).

If
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically (i.e. as in cases (i) and (ii)), then

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\lesssim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\lesssim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau).

3.6.8
The final higher order estimates

We conclude with our higher order version of Proposition
3.5.1
.

Proposition 3.6.8
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
3.5.1
,
let us assume the additional assumptions of Section
3.6.2
.

Fix
k\geq 1
.
Then for all
0<\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
and for all

\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
we have the following statement.

Let

\psi
be a solution of the inhomogeneous equation (
4.4
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
Then

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),

\displaystyle\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\left|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\}\right|+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}({\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}

(3.109)

as well as the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\left(|V^{\mu}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)_{\mu}|+|w_{p}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|\right)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F|+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}{F})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}({\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}.

(3.110)

For
p=0
, identical statements hold with
p-1
replaced by
-1-\delta
.

Remark 3.6.9
.

In the case where we replace the middle term of (
3.3
)
with (
3.10
), we
should add

\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\sqrt{\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R\}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}}

to the right hand side of (
3.110
).

Proof.

We first prove (
3.109
).
This follows from (
3.72
)
and applying
Proposition
3.6.4
(in the form of Corollary
3.6.5
)
and the properties of Section
3.6.7
.

To prove (
3.110
), let us first
commute the equation by
T^{\tilde{k}}
(and
\Omega_{1}^{\tilde{k}}
, if assumed Killing)
and apply the black box estimate (
3.54
) to
T^{\tilde{k}}\psi
(and
\Omega_{1}^{\tilde{k}}\psi
)
for
\tilde{k}\leq k
.
This gives

\displaystyle\sup_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{F}}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](v,\tau_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau)+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau^{\prime})d\tau+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

(3.111)

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathcal{E}}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau_{0})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\left(|r^{p}r^{-1}L(rT^{\tilde{k}}\psi)|+|\tilde{V}_{p}^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}T^{\tilde{k}}\psi|+|\tilde{w}_{p}\psi|\right)|T^{\tilde{k}}F|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}(T^{\tilde{k}}F)^{2},

and similarly with
\Omega_{1}^{\tilde{k}}\psi
.

Now note that in the support of
\xi
, all
\mathfrak{D}
vanish except for
T
(and
\Omega_{1}
), whence we
manifestly have

A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\sum_{\tilde{k}\leq k}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}[T^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau^{\prime})+\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$-\mkern-3.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}[\upsilon\Omega_{1}^{\tilde{k}}\psi](\tau^{\prime}),

where we recall that
\upsilon=1
only if
\Omega_{1}
is Killing.
It follows that
the left hand side of (
3.109
) is bounded by the right hand side
of (
3.110
).

Now apply the black box estimate (
3.54
) to
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
, and note that
we can bound the terms arising from
[\Box_{g_{0}},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi
by

c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

and the spacetime term involving
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\bf k}F

on the right hand side of (
3.110
).

We thus have that (
3.111
) holds with
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
in place of
T^{k}\psi
.

We apply our elliptic estimate of Proposition
3.6.6
to bound
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)
from
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)
,
generating the last term on the right hand side of (
3.110
).

Finally, by (
3.74
) of Proposition
3.6.3
, we may estimate

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\left({}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}|{\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}F|^{2}.

∎

Remark 3.6.10
.

Note that for convenience we have used (
3.109
) to obtain (
3.110
).
This was because
our precise commutation assumptions were stated with respect to the global currents.
If one does not assume estimate (
3.33
), and thus one does not have (
3.109
),
one may still obtain (
3.110
) under the assumption of asymptotic flatness of Sections
3.5

and
3.6.2
,
where we insert simply the far-away currents
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\rm far}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\rm far}
in (
3.60
).
(We also use that, from the assumptions of Section
2
, we can still define currents as in
Section
3.4.5
giving enhanced positivity
near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and in the black hole interior.)
Thus, in particular, estimate (
3.110
) holds in the Kerr case in the full subextremal range
|a|<M
.

3.6.9
Sobolev inequalities and interpolation of
p
-weighted energies

We end this section recording two easy statements about the energies we have defined.

In anticipation of studying nonlinear equations, one will need to estimate lower order pointwise quantities from higher order energies by
Sobolev inequalities.
We have the following

Proposition 3.6.11
.

Let
\psi
be a smooth function on a neighbourhood of
\Sigma(\tau)
.
Then we have

\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-3}\sup_{x\in\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{k}\psi(x))^{2}\lesssim\min\{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau),\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\}.

(3.112)

Proof.

We note that the left hand side of (
3.112
) is in fact bounded by the energy restricted to
\Sigma\cap\{r\leq R\}
, which is why
we may choose either of the two quantities on the right hand side (
3.112
).
∎

Weighted Sobolev inequalities will also hold globally on
\Sigma(\tau)
, and in practice these are used to estimate nonlinearities in the region near infinity.
Because this use is incorporated in our assumption on the null condition (see already Section
4.7
), we
shall not need to state such inequalities, although, in practice,
they will appear in the context of verifying the assumptions of
Section
4.7
. See already Appendix
C
.

Finally, we have the following easy
interpolation result:

Proposition 3.6.12
.

For
\delta
as fixed in (
3.1
), one has
the following interpolation inequalities

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\right)^{1-\delta}\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\right)^{\delta},

(3.113)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(\delta-1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\right)^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\right)^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}},

(3.114)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)}\sqrt{\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)}.

(3.115)

Proof.

The proof of these inequalities is standard and is left to the reader.
∎

4
Quasilinear equations: preliminaries, the null condition and local existence

We assume throughout that

(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
satisfies the assumptions of
Sections
2
and
3
(for cases (i), (ii) or (iii)).
We will introduce in this section the class of quasilinear equations to be considered in this
paper, and derive some preliminary results which will be used in the proof of our main theorem.

4.1
The class of equations

We will consider solutions
\psi
to quasilinear equations of the form

\Box_{g(\psi,x)}\psi=N^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\partial_{\nu}\psi,

(4.1)

where

g:\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M}\to T^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes T^{*}\mathcal{M},\qquad N:\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M}\to T\mathcal{M}\otimes T\mathcal{M}

(4.2)

are such that
\pi\circ g(\psi,x)=x
,
\pi\circ N(\psi,x)=x

where
\pi:T\mathcal{M}\otimes T\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
,
\pi:T^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes T^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}

denote the canonical projections, and
such that

g(0,x)=g_{0}(x)
for all
x
while
g(\cdot,x)=g_{0}(x)
for
r(x)\geq R/2
, and
N
and
g
are smooth maps.

We will assume moreover that, for each
k
,

\partial_{{\bf x}}^{{\bf k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}g^{\alpha\beta}(\xi,x)
and

\partial_{{\bf x}}^{{\bf k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}N^{\alpha\beta}(\xi,x)
are
uniformly bounded for all
|{\bf k}|+s\leq k
, all
r\leq R
and
|\xi|\leq 1
.
Here
\partial_{{\bf x}}^{{\bf k}}
denote multi-indices with respect to the ambient Cartesian coordinates
of Section
2.1
.

For
N^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)
, we will eventually
need in addition to assume some version of the null condition.
We will only introduce this in Section
4.7
(see already Assumption
4.7.1
).
Let us note that our assumption on the support of
g-g_{0}
is merely so as not to deal with
formulations of the null condition for the quasilinear part.

We will sometimes view the equation in (
4.1
) as an inhomogeneous equation
on a fixed
g_{0}
background, i.e. we will write it as

\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=N^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\partial_{\nu}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi,

(4.3)

and similarly its commuted versions.
We may thus apply estimates to the inhomogeneous equation

\Box_{g_{0}}\psi=F.

(4.4)

4.2
Smallness parameters

Starting in this section, we shall introduce smallness parameters (i.e. parameters related to making smallness assumptions on solutions),
denoted using the symbol
\varepsilon
and various subscripts, e.g.
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
,
\varepsilon_{\rm local}
.
Unless otherwise noted, these
will depend only on
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
,
on the nonlinearities of (
4.1
) defined by (
4.2
)
and, in general, on
k
, if there is
k
dependence in the statement.

When these smallness parameters depend on an additional quantity, this will be indicated in parenthesis, e.g.
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}(\alpha)
.

Note finally that our convention on constants denoted
C
,
c
, etc. remains the same as set in Section
3.1
, i.e. these will
not

depend on (
4.2
).

4.3
Energy currents for
\Box_{g(\psi,x)}
and the stability of coercivity properties

Let
\psi
denote a solution of (
4.1
)
on a domain
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
Because we shall use energy identities connected with
\Box_{g(\psi,x)}
, we shall
require certain basic smallness assumptions on
\psi
which ensure that the causal nature
of relevant hypersurfaces is retained and that induced volume forms of
g
and
g_{0}

are comparable.

In the sections to follow, the main smallness parameter we will consider will be
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
.
We emphasise that according to our conventions of Section
4.2
,
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
will in general depend
on the nonlinearity (
4.2
) (and
also on
k
).
The parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
can be taken as fixed everywhere in the paper, but note that
its smallness is constrained in multiple propositions whose preambles refer to its existence.

As we shall see, the propositions in this section will always refer to solutions satisfying

\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq 1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}

(4.5)

in
r\leq R
for
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
(We recall here that for
r(x)\geq R
, we have
g(\psi,x)=g_{0}
.)
Eventually, (
4.5
) will be the consequence of a stronger estimate (see already (
4.23
)).

4.3.1
Currents for
\Box_{g(x,\psi)}\psi=F
and the stability of coercivity properties

Let us for the moment consider more generally the equation

\Box_{g(\psi,x)}\psi=F

(4.6)

for arbitrary
F
, where
\psi
satisfies (
4.5
) for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
.

We may define now the currents

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}[g(\psi,x),\psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g(\psi,x),\psi],

(4.7)

again by the expressions (
3.64
), where the constituent (
3.11
), (
3.12
)
are defined with the same quadruples (
3.42
) as before,
but now with
g=g(\psi,x)
replacing
g_{0}
. These currents
satisfy (
3.15
),
with respect now to the normals and volume forms of the metric
g
, and where

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]
is defined by (
3.13
). (Notice that the definition of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]
does not depend on the
metric.) In the region
r\geq R
of course, all currents coincide with their
g_{0}
versions,
since
g=g_{0}
in this region.

We have the following

Proposition 4.3.1
.

There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that
the following statement holds.

Let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.6
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.5
) for
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
. Then
x\mapsto g(\psi(x),x)
defines a Lorentzian metric
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
and the identity (
3.15
) holds in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)
, for all
v
such that
\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)
,
where
the coefficients, normals and volume forms
are
\varepsilon^{1/4}
close to those of the currents (
4.7
) corresponding to
\Box_{g_{0}}\phi=F
.

In particular, there exist constants
C
,
c
, such that, for
p=0
or
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, the corresponding
coercivity properties (
3.45
)
are retained for the currents (
4.7
), while (
3.43
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}[g(\psi,x),\psi]+\tilde{A}\xi(r)\psi^{2}

\displaystyle\geq cr^{p-1}\rho(r)\left(({L}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)+cr^{-1-\delta}\rho(r)(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+cr^{-3}\tilde{\rho}(r)\psi^{2}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\qquad-C\varepsilon^{1/4}\,\iota_{\{r\leq R\}\cap\{\rho\leq C\varepsilon^{1/4}<\frac{1}{2}\}}\left((L\psi)^{2}+(\underline{L}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+\psi^{2}\right).

In particular, we still have (
3.43
)
in
r\geq R
, and also in
\{r\leq R\}\cap\{\rho\geq C\varepsilon^{1/4}\}.

Given
\varsigma=\varsigma(k)
as fixed previously,
then the above applies also for the currents
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\varsigma}[g(\psi,x),\psi]
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\varsigma}[g(\psi,x),\psi]
in the region
r\geq r_{\rm Killing}
,
while in the region
r\leq r_{1}(\varsigma)
,
the coercivity properties of Propositions
3.4.1
and
3.4.2
hold for these currents.

Proof.

This is clear from the properties of tensor identities. Note how the smallness constraint on
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
provided by this
proposition
depends of course on the map
g
of (
4.2
) and is needed even simply to ensure for
g
to be Lorentzian and for the inverse metric to be well defined.
∎

Note that since
\rho=\tilde{\rho}=1
in case (i), the bound (
4.8
) implies
that the full coercivity applies in that case. (Note that if
\rho
is a step function valued in
\{0,1\}
, then
\{\rho\geq C\varepsilon^{1/4}\}=\{\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}\}=\{\rho=1\}
and
\{\rho\leq C\varepsilon^{1/4}\}=\{\rho=0\}
.)

Corollary 4.3.2
.

Under the assumption of Proposition
4.3.1
, we have the coercivity statement

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g(\psi,x),\psi]\geq\frac{8}{9}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g_{0},\psi]

in
\{r\leq r_{2}\}\cup\{r\geq R/4\}
and an analogue of the coercivity statement of (
3.85
) holds in the form

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}

\displaystyle-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\geq r_{2}\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{0}}]\psi\}|

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}\cap\{r\geq R/4\}}\frac{2}{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g(\psi,x),\psi]+C\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(F+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi))^{2}.

We have moreover the following version of (
3.103
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g(\psi,x),\psi]+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\tilde{A}\xi(r)(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},\qquad

\displaystyle 2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{K}}[g(\psi,x),\psi]+\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\tilde{A}\xi(r)(\mathfrak{D}^{k}\psi)^{2}
