\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},\qquad

\displaystyle p=0.

Proof.

Note that we have retained
g_{0}
on the left hand side of (
4.9
),
so this is simply a statement about the stability of coercivity properties of the first term on the right hand side.
∎

4.3.2
Relations of energy fluxes

In view of the above we will define a new set of energy quantities,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(g)
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g)
,
etc., defined in parallel with those of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}

of Section
3.6.7
(to be denoted below as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(g_{0})
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g_{0})
),
but where the flux is defined
with respect to the divergence identity with respect to
g=g(\psi,x)
, i.e. we define

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g,\tau):=\int_{\Sigma(\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[g,\psi]n(g)_{\Sigma(\tau)}^{\mu},\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(g,\tau):=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[g,\psi]n(g)_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mu},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(g,v,\tau_{0},\tau):=\int_{\underline{C}_{v}\cap\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{J}}_{\mu}[g,\psi]n(g)_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu},\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau(v)

where the omitted volume forms above are here understood with respect to
g=g(\psi,x)
.

Corollary 4.3.3
.

We have that under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3.1
,
for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
,
v
such that
\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)
,

\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g,\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g_{0},\tau)\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(g,\tau)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathcal{S}}(g,\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(g_{0},\tau)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(g_{0},\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau),

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(g,v,\tau_{0},\tau)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(g_{0},v,\tau_{0},\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),

in fact

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g)\leq(1+C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}})\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g_{0}),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g_{0})\leq(1+C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}})\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g),

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}[g](\tau)+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r_{1}\leq r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(F+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi))^{2}.

(4.12)

As always, the significance of expressing the above with respect to the energies
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(g)
, etc., is that the constants in our nonlinear
inequalities will be exactly
1
.

Note that in all integrals below with volume form omitted, we will continue to use the volume form induced by
g_{0}
unless otherwise noted.
(We shall only use the volume form induced by
g(\psi,x)
when applying the divergence identity involving (
4.11
).
The corresponding volume forms are of course equivalent under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3.1
,
but again one must distinguish where appropriate so as to obtain the exact constant.)

4.4
Higher order estimates for the quasilinear equation

Using the above, we finally
obtain the following:

Proposition 4.4.1
.

For all
k\geq 1
.
There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that
the following statement holds.

Let
0<\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
,

\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}
and
let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.5
)
for
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
. Then for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+c\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\left|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\partial_{\beta}\psi)\}\right|

(4.13)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\left|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\Box_{g(\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{0}},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi\}\right|

(4.14)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}({{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\partial_{\beta}\psi))^{2}+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\left([\Box_{g(\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{0}},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi))^{2}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\boxed{\varepsilon^{1/4}}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}\cap\{\rho\leq C\sqrt{\varepsilon}\}}\sum_{|{\bf{k}}|\leq k}\left((L(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+(\underline{L}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi))^{2}+|\not{\nabla}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\bf k}}\psi)|^{2}\right)+\psi^{2}

(4.16)

as well as the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(|V^{\mu}_{p}\partial_{\mu}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)|+|w_{p}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi)|

(4.18)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}\left({{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi)\right)^{2}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-2}\left({\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi)\right)^{2}

(4.20)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-2}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi)\right)^{2}.

(4.21)

Moreover, for
p=0
, identical statements hold with
-1-\delta
replacing
p-1
.

Remark 4.4.2
.

In the case where we replace the middle term of (
3.3
)
with (
3.10
), we
should add

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}\sqrt{\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|^{2}}\,\,\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\,\sqrt{\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R\}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi)^{2}}

to the right hand side of (
4.18
). Cf. Remark
3.6.9
.

Proof.

Note the term on line (
4.14
) arose by expanding the term appearing in the actual identity as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\Box_{g(\psi,x)},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\Box_{g(\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{0}},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{H}}[\psi]\cdot\{[\Box_{g_{0}},\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}]\psi\}

and then bringing the second term to the left hand side to use
Corollary
4.3.2
and absorb it in the bulk. (Recall that in the region
r\geq R
, we have
g=g_{0}
.)
The term on line (
4.15
)
arose from our application of elliptic estimates to
\psi
, considering
it as a solution of (
4.3
).
The term on line (
4.16
) arose from the term on the last line
of (
4.8
).
We note that restricted to
r\geq R
, we may replace
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}
commutation with

\mathfrak{D}
commutation.

The inequality (
4.17
)–(
4.21
), on the other hand,
simply arises from applying the estimate (
3.110
) to the nonlinear equation
written in the form (
4.3
). Note that we have applied it at one order less, i.e. with
k-1

in place of
k
, in view of the fact that the right hand side is of order
k
.
∎

4.5
Summed norm notation and the lower order smallness assumption

In addition to the primitive assumption (
4.5
),
in the context of our proof,
we will need to introduce stronger a priori
energy smallness assumptions on
\psi
in our region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
under consideration.
In the context of the proof of the main theorem, this will appear as a bootstrap assumption.
This will ensure that higher order nonlinear terms are indeed
absorbable.

We first explain some additional notation.
We define the following summed quantities for
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq v(\tau)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left(\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left(\,\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}.

For
p=0
we first define the analogous quantities, where
p-1
is replaced by
-1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left(\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left(\,\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}.

Because
p=0
is anomalous, however, we will need in addition the following stronger energies which will appear
on the right hand side
of
p=0
estimates:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(\delta-1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left({}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(\delta-1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{v:\tau_{1}\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\left({}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(\delta-1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(\delta-1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}.

Note the general properties that
p^{\prime}\geq p
,
k^{\prime}\geq k

implies
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
,
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\gtrsim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
,

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\gtrsim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
, while

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}.

(4.22)

We will use the notation
\ll\mkern-6.0muk
to denote some particular positive integer, depending on
k
,
which may be different on different instances of our use of the notation,
such that
\ll\mkern-6.0muk\leq k
, and in fact,
\ll\mkern-6.0muk
is “much less than
k
”,
provided
k
is sufficiently
large. In particular, for all positive integers
n
,
we assume there exists a
k(n)
for which
k\geq k(n)
implies
\ll\mkern-6.0muk\leq k-n
.

We have the following:

Proposition 4.5.1
.

Let
k\geq 4
be sufficiently large. There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that for all
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
, the following holds.

Let
\psi
satisfy

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\leq\varepsilon

(4.23)

in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, for
p=0
(or some
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
),
where
\ll\mkern-6.0muk\geq 4
.
Then the following improved version of (
4.5
) holds:

\sup_{r\leq R}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq 1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}

(4.24)

Proof.

This follows of course immediately from the Sobolev inequality (
3.112
).
∎

In the context of the proof of the main theorem,
inequality
(
4.23
) will be introduced as a
bootstrap assumption, and Proposition
4.5.1
will be applied to
retrieve the assumption (
4.5
),
necessary for the
results of Sections
4.3
–
4.4
. Note that with the assumption (
4.23
),
we may replace the boxed
\varepsilon^{1/4}
in (
4.16
) with
\sqrt{\varepsilon}
.

We note that in what follows, restrictions on
k
sufficiently large will always include the condition
\ll\mkern-6.0muk\geq 4
.

4.5.1
Comparability of
\mathfrak{E}
and
\mathcal{E}
energies

We note first the following:

Proposition 4.5.2
.

Let
k\geq 4
be sufficiently large. There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that the following holds.

Let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.23
) for
p=0
and some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
Then setting
F=N^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\partial_{\nu}\psi+(\Box_{g_{0}}-\Box_{g(\psi,x)})\psi
,
we have

\int_{\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r\leq R\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}F)^{2}\lesssim\min\left\{\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\,,\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right\}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\varepsilon\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime}).

(4.25)

Proof.

This is standard in view of our assumptions on
N
and
g(\psi,x)
from Section
4.1
,
and can be proven using the Sobolev inequality (
3.112
)
on
\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})\cap\{r\leq R\}
.
∎

We may now obtain the following result, which can be viewed as a corollary of
Proposition
3.6.6
.

Corollary 4.5.3
.

Let
k\geq 4
be sufficiently large. Then
there exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that
the following statement holds.

Let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.23
) for
p=0
and some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
We have the analogue of Corollary
3.6.7
:
The calligraphic and fraktur (when applicable) energies are comparable, i.e.

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)(g),

for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
.
Moreover, if
\chi=1
and
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically (as in case (i)), then

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau),\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

and thus

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

If
\tilde{\rho}=1
identically,
\rho=\chi
, (i.e. as in cases (i) and (ii)), then

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau),\qquad{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)\sim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)+{}^{\tilde{\rho}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-3\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)

and thus

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

Proof.

This follows from (
3.104
)–(
3.108
), with
F
defined as in Proposition
4.5.2
,
absorbing the inhomogeneous term using the estimate (
4.25
), for sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
∎

4.6
Estimates on the nonlinear terms in the near region

Proposition 4.6.1
.

Let
k\geq 4
be sufficiently large.
There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
such that the following holds.

Let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.23
)
for
p=0
and some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
Then all integrals on lines (
4.13
)–(
4.16
),

restricted to
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R\}
,
may be estimated by

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime},

(4.26)

while the integrals on lines (
4.18
)–(
4.19
),
again
restricted to
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R\}
,
with or without the extra term of Remark
4.4.2
,
may be similarly estimated by

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime}.

(4.27)

Proof.

This is standard given the assumptions in Section
4.1
and can be proven using the Sobolev inequality (
3.112
)
on
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}
, cf. the proof of (
4.25
) which in fact already bounds several of the terms.
(Note that the boxed
\varepsilon^{1/4}
in (
4.16
)
may clearly now be replaced by the quantity
\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})}
within the integral.)
∎

We remark again that, as opposed to (
4.26
),
the control (
4.27
)
loses a derivative, as the energy on the left hand side of (
4.18
) is

k-1
’th order while the right hand side of (
4.27
)
is of
k
’th order.

4.7
Assumptions for the nonlinearity in the far region: the “null” condition

We may now state our
specific assumption capturing the null condition for the semilinear terms.

Rather than formulate algebraically the null condition in terms of the form of
N
, for maximum generality we will make our basic assumption directly
at the level of an estimate for terms on the right hand side of the inequalities of Proposition
4.4.1
.

Our assumptions will only depend on the region
r\geq 8R/9
, so we will need some notation to denote the restriction of energies, etc., to this region.
Given
r_{*}\geq r_{0}
and
v
, by

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{*},v},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v},\qquad\ldots

we shall mean the expressions defined as before, but where all domains of integration are restricted to the region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq r_{*}\}
.
We may thus define these expressions for functions
\psi:\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq r_{*}\}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Similarly, the above expressions without the
v
subscript, e.g.
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{*}}
, will be defined where all domains of intergation
are restricted to
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\geq r_{*}\}
. These can then be defined for functions

\psi:\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\geq r_{*}\}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Let us note that

r_{*}\geq 8R/9\implies\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v}={}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v}={}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v}

(4.28)

and similarly for the quantities without the
v
subscript.

Assumption 4.7.1

(Null condition for semilinear terms)
.

There exists a
k_{\rm null}
, and for all
k\geq k_{\rm null}
,
an
\varepsilon_{\rm null}>0
, such that the following holds for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}
,
v
.

Let
\psi
be a smooth function defined on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq 8R/9\}

satisfying the bound

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{8R/9,v}\leq\varepsilon

(4.29)

for
some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{null}
.
Then for all
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}(|r^{p}r^{-1}L(r{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)|+|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi))^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})},

(4.30)

while, corresponding to
p=0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}(|r^{-1}L(r{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)|+|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi))^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

(4.31)

We note the following

Proposition 4.7.2
.

Assumption
4.7.1
holds for equations (
4.1
)
where
g_{0}
is Minkowski and where the semilinear terms
N
satisfy
the classical null condition of Klainerman
[
Kla86
]
, and more generally
when
g_{0}
is the Kerr metric and
the semilinear terms
N
belong to the class considered by Luk
[
Luk13
]
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C
.
∎

Remark 4.7.3
.

Let us note that for
\psi
defined on the slab
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\cap\{r\geq 8R/9\}
,
from the trivial bound
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*},v}\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*}}
we may drop the
v
subscripts.
For
\psi
defined globally on
the slab
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
, then
in view of (
4.28
) and the trivial bounds

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{r_{*}}\leq{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
, etc.,
we may replace the
\frac{8M}{9},v
subscripts in all the
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
, etc., energies on the right hand side with
the
\rho
superscript, i.e. replace
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8M}{9},v}
, etc., with
{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
, etc.
Also, for such
\psi
, we may clearly replace (
4.29
) with assumption (
4.23
).

4.8
The final estimates

Putting everything together we have the following:

Proposition 4.8.1
.

Consider
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})

satisfying the assumptions of Sections
2
and
3

(for cases (i), (ii) or (iii))
and equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
and
4.7
.

Then for all
k\geq 4
sufficiently large, there exist
constants
C>0
(also implicit in the
\lesssim
below),
c>0

and an
\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}>0
,
such that the following is true.

Let
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}
and
let
\psi
be a solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.23
) with
p=0
for some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.
Then for
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, one has the estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),

\displaystyle\quad c\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+C\,\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime}

(4.32)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime}

(4.33)

while for
p=0
, one has the estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{v:\tau\leq\tau(v)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}(v,\tau_{0},\tau),\quad

\displaystyle c\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{1})\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+A\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\xi$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+C\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime},

(4.34)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime}.

(4.35)

Finally, in both the case
p=0
and the case
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, one has
the alternative estimate

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+(1+\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{{}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})},

(4.36)

depending on
\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.4.1
, Corollary
4.5.3
, Proposition
4.6.1
and
Assumption
4.7.1
.
∎

Remark 4.8.2
.

Note how the case
p=0
is anomalous in that one sees
0+
on the right hand side
of (
4.34
)–(
4.35
).
It is this fact which will require us to use
p>0
weights to close the
global estimates (
4.32
)–(
4.33
),
even in the case (i). On the other hand, (
4.36
) is sufficient to show local existence
even using only the
p=0
weight.

Remark 4.8.3
.

In using the above we shall always simply replace terms like

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, etc., by
{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, etc., according to Remark
4.7.3
. We have kept the terms in the above form
simply to highlight their origin in the estimate of Assumption
4.7.1
.

Remark 4.8.4
.

In the case where we only assume the black box inequality (
3.3
) and
the assumptions of asymptotic flatness of Sections
3.5

and
3.6.2
as interpreted in Remark
3.6.10
(necessary
to obtain (
3.110
))
and moreover, the equation (
4.1
) is semilinear, i.e.
g(x,\psi)=g_{0}
for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
,
then we obtain the statement of Proposition
4.8.1
without inequalities (
4.32
) and (
4.34
)
and where (
4.33
) and (
4.35
) are
replaced by

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime},

(4.37)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\,\,}d\tau^{\prime},

(4.38)

respectively.
(In particular, equation (
4.36
) also still holds as stated.)

4.9
Local well posedness

Finally, we give a local well posedness statement and an extension criterion.

Since our initial data hypersurfaces
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
are in part null, it will be convenient to assume that our
initial data are smooth.
We define a smooth initial data set on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})

to be a pair
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
where
\uppsi
is a function on

\Sigma(\tau_{0})
smooth on

\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
and smooth on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\geq R\}
, and

\uppsi^{\prime}
is a smooth function on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
,
such that moreover there exists a smooth a function
\Psi
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

for some
\tau_{1}
such that
\Psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})}=\uppsi
, and
n\Psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}}=\uppsi^{\prime}
,
where
n
denotes the normal to
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
.

Note that given such initial data on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
one may define

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]

(4.39)

as follows:

Using the equation (
4.1
) we may
compute along
\Sigma(\tau_{0})

the
k+1
-jet of any smooth solution
\psi
of (
4.1
) such that

\psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})}=\uppsi,\qquad n\psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}}=\uppsi^{\prime}.

(4.40)

We may define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]
to equal the usual
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})
where the derivatives
are interpreted in terms of this formal computation.
Note of course that the expression (
4.39
) may well be infinite.

Alternatively, we may express this as follows. Using the above computation, we may
in fact define a smooth
\Psi
as in the previous paragraph, such that

\Psi
moreover satisfies (
4.1
) along
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
,
and,
for all
|{\bf k}|\leq k
,

\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\Psi
satisfies along
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
the equation one obtains by commuting (
4.1
) by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}
.
We may then simply define (
4.39
) substituting this
\Psi
for
\psi
in the usual expression
for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})
.

With this, we can now state our local well posedness:

Proposition 4.9.1

(Local well posedness)
.

Consider
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})

satisfying the assumptions of Sections
2
and
3

(for cases (i), (ii) or (iii))
and equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
and
4.7
. Fix either
p=0
or

\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
.

There exists a positive integer
k_{\rm loc}\geq 4
such that the following holds. Let
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
.
Then there exists a positive real constant
C>0
sufficiently large,
a positive real parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}>0
sufficiently small, and a decreasing positive function
\tau_{\rm exist}:(0,\varepsilon_{\rm loc})\to\mathbb{R}

such that for all smooth initial data
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
such that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc},

there exists a smooth solution
\psi

of (
4.1
)
in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
for
\tau_{1}:=\tau_{0}+\tau_{\rm exist}
which satisfies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\leq C\varepsilon_{0}.

Moreover, for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
,
any other smooth
\widetilde{\psi}
defined on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
satisfying (
4.1
) in

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
and attaining the initial data, i.e. satisfying (
4.40
) (with
\widetilde{\psi}

replacing
\psi
), coincides with the restriction of
\psi
, i.e.
\widetilde{\psi}=\psi|_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}
.

We have in addition the following propagation of higher order regularity and/or higher weighted estimates.
Given
p
,
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
,
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
,
\tau_{0}
,
\tau_{1}
as above,

2-\delta\geq p^{\prime}\geq p
if
p\geq\delta
and either
2-\delta\geq p^{\prime}\geq\delta
or
p^{\prime}=0
if
p=0
, and
k^{\prime}\geq k
. Then there exists a constant
C(k^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})
such that
given
\psi
as above,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\leq C(k^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0}).

Proof.

This can be easily proven using estimate (
4.36
).
We leave the details to the reader.
∎

Note also the following easy corollary, which we will use as an extension criterion:

Corollary 4.9.2

(Continuation criterion)
.

Fix
p=0
or
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, and let
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}

be as in Proposition
4.9.1
. There exists a constant
C>0
and an
\epsilon>0
such that the following is true.

Let
\tau_{f}>\tilde{\tau}_{0}
and suppose that there exists a smooth solution
\psi
of (
4.1
)
on

\cup_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}<\tau<\tau_{f}}\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tau)

such that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}

for
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
and all
\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tau<\tau_{f}
. Then, defining
\tau_{1}:=\tau_{f}+\epsilon
,

\psi
extends uniquely to a smooth function
on
\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tau_{1})
satisfying the equation (
4.1
) on this set
and, setting
\tau_{0}:=\max\{\tau_{f}-\epsilon,\tilde{\tau}_{0}\}
, satisfying the estimate

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm loc}.

(4.41)

Moreover, for all
k^{\prime}\geq k
and all
2-\delta\geq p^{\prime}\geq p
,
there exist constants
C(k^{\prime})
such that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\leq C(k^{\prime})\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0}).

(4.42)

Remark 4.9.3
.

Recall that by our conventions the constants
C
,
k_{\rm loc}
,
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
depend in addition on
k
.
We may assume that
k_{\rm loc}
is sufficiently large so that
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
satisfies the largeness constraint
of all previous propositions in this Section, and that
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm prelim}
.

Remark 4.9.4
.

In view of Remark
4.8.4
, and the fact that one needs only (
4.36
),
all the statements of this section also hold in the semilinear case,
under the relaxed assumptions described there.

5
The main theorem: global existence and stability

We may now state the main result of this paper.

Theorem 5.1

(Global existence and stability)
.

Consider
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})

satisfying the assumptions of Sections
2
and
3

(for cases (i), (ii) or (iii))
and equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
and Assumption
4.7.1

of Section
4.7
.

There exists a positive integer
k_{\rm global}\geq k_{\rm local}
sufficiently large,
such that, given
k\geq k_{\rm global}
, there exists a positive
0<\varepsilon_{\rm global}<\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
sufficiently small,
and a positive constant
C>0
sufficiently large, so that the following holds.

Fix
\tau_{0}=1

and consider as in Proposition
4.9.1

initial data
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
for (
4.1
) satisfying

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global},

according to case (i), (ii) and (iii) respectively, where in the former case
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
.
Then the
\psi
given by Proposition
4.9.1

extends to a unique globally defined solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)
,
satisfying the estimates

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},

(5.1)

according to cases (i), (ii), (iii), respectively,
for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
, in particular

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq C\varepsilon_{0},

respectively.

The solution will satisfy moreover estimates (
6.8
) in case (i), estimates (
6.62
)
and (
6.64
)–(
6.68
) in
case (ii) and estimates (
6.116
)–(
6.121
) in case (iii).

We shall give the proof of this theorem in Section
6
.

Remark 5.1
.

Our theorem also applies
under the relaxed assumptions of Remark
4.8.4
,
where (
4.1
) is however required to be semilinear. Here we can again distinguish case (i) where
\chi=1
and
case (ii) where
\chi
is allowed to degenerate. In case (i), the theorem holds as stated except for (
6.8
), which
no longer applies. Similarly, in case (ii), the theorem holds as stated except for (
6.62
) which no longer applies.
The necessary modifications will be collected in a series of remarks in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
.
(See already Section
6.2.5
for the completion of case (ii).)

We reiterate (cf. Propositions
4.7.2
, Theorem
A.1
and Theorem
D.1
) that the above theorem in particular holds for equations (
4.1
) on the Kerr spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
for
|a|\ll M
, with quasilinear terms
as described in Section
4.1
and the general semilinear terms considered in
[
Luk13
]
, and, by the above remark,
restricted to the purely semilinear case,
for the full range of parameters
|a|<M
.

6
The estimate hierarchies and global existence and decay

We
proceed with the proof of Theorem
5.1
in cases (i), (ii) and (iii). These will
be treated in Sections
6.1
,
6.2

and
6.3
below, respectively.

Note that we will now suppress some of the cumbersome notation with the following conventions:

In this section, the fraktur energies
\mathfrak{E}
,
\mathfrak{F}
will everywhere
denote
\mathfrak{E}(g)
,
\mathfrak{F}(g)
, so we will drop explicit reference to
g
here.
When the region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
is assumed, we will omit the
\tau_{0}
or
(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
arguments
in various energies, etc.,
and denote
\mathcal{X}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
by
\mathcal{X}
.

The smallness parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
will be determined in the context of the proof.
We will in fact introduce other parameters on the way; these will be in the relation

\varepsilon_{\rm global}\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm slab}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc},

(6.1)

where parameters
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}
,
\varepsilon_{\rm slab}
will only appear for cases (ii) and (iii).
The parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
will be used in the context of a continuity or bootstrap argument. Let us remark already that
if the basic bootstrap estimate (
4.23
) holds for an
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
, then by Remark
4.9.3
,

\varepsilon
will satisfy the smallness requirements of all propositions of Section
4
, for the regions under consideration.

6.1
Case (i)

Case (i) is the most elementary case, where, moreover only the minimal nontrivial

r^{p}
weights for
2-\delta\geq p\geq\delta

are required on data. (For instance, one may fix
p=\delta
.)
We present first the fundamental estimate in Section
6.1.1
that holds
under the bootstrap assumption (
4.23
) and then
carry out the global existence proof in Section
6.1.2
.

The proof will also hold for the semilinear case
with the relaxed assumptions of Remark
4.8.4
, if we are in the analogue of case (i), i.e. if
\chi=1
.
The modifications necessary to treat this case are described in a series of remarks (see already
Remarks
6.1.2
and
6.1.3
).

6.1.1
The fundamental estimate

The fundamental estimate is given simply by the following:

Proposition 6.1.1
.

Let
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
and let
k
be sufficiently large, and let us assume the case (i) assumptions.
There exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}>0
sufficiently small so that the following is true.

Let
\psi
solve (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, satisfying
moreover (
4.23
) with our chosen
p
and with
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
. Then we have:

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0}),\qquad\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})(1+C\varepsilon^{1/2}).

(6.2)

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
. The assumption of Proposition
4.8.1
is satisfied in view
of our discussion following (
6.1
).
From estimate (
4.32
) of
Proposition
4.8.1
, since in case (i) we have
\rho=1
,
A=0
,
\tilde{A}=0
, it follows that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}},\qquad\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+C\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}.

(6.3)

By our bootstrap assumption (
4.23
), we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\leq\varepsilon.

(6.4)

Thus, possibly requiring
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
to be even smaller, we may absorb the nonlinear term in the first inequality
of (
6.3
) to obtain the first inequality of (
6.2
).

To obtain the second inequality of (
6.2
), we remark that Corollary
4.5.3

applies to yield in particular

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0}).

(6.5)

The second inequality of (
6.2
) now immediately follows from the
the second inequality of (
6.3
), plugging in the
first inequality of (
6.2
)
just established, (
6.5
)
and (
6.4
).
∎

Remark 6.1.2
.

Under the relaxed assumptions of Remark
4.8.4
,
where (
4.1
) is however required to be semilinear and we are in the analogue of case (i), i.e.
\chi=1
,
then from (
4.38
) we obtain the first inequality of (
6.3
). Thus, the above proposition
holds as stated for the first inequality of (
6.2
).

6.1.2
Proof of Theorem
5.1
in case (i)

We now carry out the proof of Theorem
5.1
proper in case (i).

Let
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
be as in the statement and
recall the assumption

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}

on initial data, for a sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
to be determined.

Consider
the set
\mathfrak{B}
consisting of all
\tau_{f}>\tau_{0}
such that a solution
\psi
of (
4.1
)
obtaining the data exists on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{f})
,
and such that moreover
the energy bootstrap assumption (
4.23
)
holds on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}:=\tau_{f})
with our chosen
p
and where
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
is chosen to
satisfy

1\gg\varepsilon\gg\varepsilon_{\rm global}.

(6.6)

(The above relation in particular constrains
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
to be small.)
By the local well posedness statement Proposition
4.9.1
,
it follows
that since
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
and
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
,
we have that
\tau_{0}+\tau_{\rm exist}\in\mathfrak{B}
and thus
\mathfrak{B}\neq\emptyset
.
Also note that a fortiori, if
\tau_{f}\in\mathfrak{B}
, then
(\tau_{0},\tau_{f}]\subset\mathfrak{B}

and thus
\mathfrak{B}
is manifestly a connected subset of
(\tau_{0},\infty)
.

For any
\tau_{1}\in\mathfrak{B}
, Proposition
6.1.1
applies in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
The first estimate of (
6.2
) then yields

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.7)

If follows that
for sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\rm glob}
, and all
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm glob}
,
the continuation criterion Corollary
4.9.2

applies to obtain that
\psi
extends as a solution to (
4.1
)
on some
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{f}+\epsilon)
, where
\epsilon
is independent of
\tau_{f}
, and that, considering now
\tau_{1}:=\tau_{f}+\epsilon
,
inequality (
6.7
) holds also in

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}:=\tau_{f}+\epsilon)

(with a slightly different implicit constant than that of (
6.7
)).
(To infer this from (
4.41
), which was an estimate on
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{f},\tau_{f}+\epsilon)
, note
that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{f})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{f},\tau_{f}+\epsilon)
.)

Finally we note that for
\varepsilon
satisfying (
6.6
),
inequality (
6.7
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}:=\tau_{f}+\epsilon)

shows in particular that inequalities (
4.23
) hold in this set.
It follows by the definition of
\mathfrak{B}
that
\tau_{f}+\epsilon\in\mathfrak{B}
and thus, given also its connectedness, the set
\mathfrak{B}
is open.
Since
\epsilon
does not depend on
\tau_{f}
, it follows that
\mathfrak{B}
is also closed, as any limit point
\tau_{\rm limit}

of
\mathfrak{B}
in
(\tau_{0},\infty)
satisfies
\tau_{\rm limit}\leq\tau_{f}+\epsilon
for some
\tau_{f}\in\mathfrak{B}
.
We have shown that
\mathfrak{B}
is a non-empty open and closed subset of
(\tau_{0},\infty)
and thus,

\mathfrak{B}=(\tau_{0},\infty)
. Hence the solution
\psi

exists globally in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)
and
satisfies (
6.7
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
where
\tau_{1}
is now
any
\tau_{1}>\tau_{0}
. This gives (
5.1
).

Revisiting (
6.2
)
we see finally that we have the following more precise global estimate:

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)\leq\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})(1+C\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}).

(6.8)

This completes the proof.

Remark 6.1.3
.

Note that in view of Remark
6.1.2
,
the above proof goes through,
except for the last paragraph involving (
6.8
),
also under the relaxed assumptions of Remark
4.8.4
,
where (
4.1
) is however required to be semilinear and we are in the analogue of case (i), i.e.
\chi=1
.
Thus, in this case, one indeed obtains the statement as given in Remark
5.1
.

6.2
Case (ii)

Case (ii) is the second simplified case which we shall consider.
Unlike case (i), we shall require proving actual
\tau
-decay above a certain threshold in order to close, and this
in turn will require raising
p
to
p=2-\delta
in our initial energy assumptions.

The logic of the proof is a simplified version of the scheme we shall use for the general case
and which has already been summarised in the introduction:

1.

Rather than bootstrap directly
t
-weighted estimates, we formulate the fundamental estimates, depending only on
the bootstrap assumption (
4.23
),
as a hierarchy of estimates on a spacetime slab of
\tau
-length at most
L
.
This is the content of Section
6.2.1
.

2.

We shall then show by a bootstrap argument,
restricted to such a slab
, how global existence
and estimates on the slab can be proven, with suitable assumptions on the initial data of the slab, which now
involve
L
. This is the content of Section
6.2.2
.

3.

Still restricted to a given slab of length
L
, we will
show that one can in fact a posteriori improve the above estimates
under suitable additional assumptions on the initial data,
and, using a pigeonhole argument, show moreover an improved estimate
for any
\Sigma(\tau^{\prime})
slice near the top of
the slab. This is the content of Section
6.2.3
.

4.

Finally, global existence and
\tau
decay now follow by iterating the above estimates on
a consecutive sequence of spacetime slabs of dyadic time-length
L_{i}=2^{i}
. This is the content of Section
6.2.4
.

We shall in addition give an alternative iteration proof in Section
6.2.5
, which does not use the exact boundedness
statement of the fraktur energies.
The advantage of this alternative proof is that it allows us to treat the semilinear case with the relaxed assumptions of Remark
4.8.4
.
The modifications necessary to treat this case are described in a series of remarks (see already
Remarks
6.2.2
,
6.2.4
,
6.2.7
and
6.2.8
).

6.2.1
The hierarchy of inequalities

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
k
be sufficiently large and let us assume the case (ii) assumptions.
There exist constants
C>0
,
c>0
and an
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}>0
sufficiently small
such that the following is true.

Consider a region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and a
\psi
solving (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, satisfying
moreover (
4.23
) with
p=0
and with
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
.
Let us assume moreover that

\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{0}+L

for some arbitrary
L>0
.
We have the following hierarchy of inequalities on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{1}),\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{1}),\quad c\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+C\,\left({}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sqrt{{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\right)+C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L},

(6.9)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{1}),\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{1}),\quad c\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L},

(6.10)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{F}}}(v,\tau_{1}),\quad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{1}),\quad c\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+C\left(\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}.

Proof.

Again we recall that if
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
, then the assumption of Proposition
4.8.1
holds.
The first two inequalities follow from the estimate (
4.32
) of
Proposition
4.8.1

applied to
p=2-\delta
,
p=1

in view of the fact that

\rho=\chi
, where we have used
the relations (
4.22
)
and (
4.28
) to replace the far-away supported nonlinear terms with those displayed above,
together with the estimate

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})}d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\sqrt{\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\,\cdot\,\sqrt{L}\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}.

Note that for the
p=1
estimate we retain only the less precise expression
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}
which will be sufficient for our purposes.
