{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{-2+\delta}+\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}L^{-\frac{3}{2}\frac{(1-\delta)(\gamma+2\delta)}{1+\delta}}.

(6.96)

The inequalities (
6.94
) give the first second and fourth inequality of (
6.89
).

Note that
-2+\delta<-\frac{3}{2}+\delta
.
We may however improve (
6.96
) iteratively as follows:
If

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{-\gamma},

for
\gamma\leq 2-\delta
,
then

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{-\gamma}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}L^{-\gamma},

hence, plugging this again into (
6.81
),
we obtain

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{-2+\delta}+\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}L^{-\frac{3}{2}\frac{(1-\delta)(\gamma+2\delta)}{1+\delta}}.

(6.97)

Setting
\gamma_{0}=\frac{3}{2}\frac{(1-\delta)(1+2\delta)}{1+\delta}
, and defining inductively

\gamma_{i}=\frac{3}{2}\frac{(1-\delta)(\gamma_{i-1}+2\delta)}{1+\delta}
,
we have that there exists a first
i\geq 1
such that
\frac{3}{2}\frac{(1-\delta)(\gamma_{i}+2\delta)}{1+\delta}>2-\delta
.
It follows that (
6.97
) holds for
\gamma=\gamma_{i}
, hence

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{-2+\delta}.

This yields (
6.90
).
Note that this implies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L^{(-2+2\delta)z}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}L^{-2+\delta}.

(6.98)

On the other hand, the fourth inequality of (
6.89
) implies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L^{-1+\delta}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}L^{-1+\delta}.

(6.99)

We may now infer the third inequality of (
6.89
) by plugging in the derived bounds into (
6.95
).
This concludes the proof of (
6.89
).

To show (
6.91
)–(
6.93
), respectively, we first apply the pigeonhole principle as in
[
DR09
]

to
the inequality

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+L}\left(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\alpha^{-1}L^{1-\delta}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0},

which, upon addition, follows from the inequalities of the estimate (
6.89
) already shown.
Recalling from (
3.93
) that we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},

we obtain
that there exists
\tau^{\prime\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+L/2]
, whose precise value depends on the solution, such that

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\cdot L^{-1},

(6.100)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\cdot L^{-1},

(6.101)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L^{-1+\delta}\cdot L^{-1}.

(6.102)

Now in view of the interpolation estimate (
3.113
) of Proposition
3.6.12
,
we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\lesssim\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{1-\delta}\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{\delta}\leq\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{1-\delta}\left(\,\,\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{0}+L}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\right)^{\delta}

(6.103)

and thus

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}L^{-1+\delta},

(6.104)

where we have used (
6.101
) and
the estimate for the second factor on the right hand side of (
6.103
) contained in the second inequality of (
6.89
).

Now
we apply (
6.80
)
and (
6.81
) again, with
\tau^{\prime\prime}
in place of
\tau_{0}
,
using (
6.100
)–(
6.102
) to bound the initial data,
to obtain that
for all
\tau_{0}+L\geq\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau_{0}+L/2
, we have

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}L^{-1},

(6.105)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}L^{-1+\delta},

(6.106)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L^{-2+\delta}.

(6.107)

Thus, in view of the requirement
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}\gg 1
, for sufficiently large
\alpha_{0}
we may
absorb the constants implicit in
\lesssim
by explicit constants of our choice by adding extra positive
\alpha

powers to the right hand side of (
6.106
) and (
6.107
).
In this way, we obtain the specific
estimates (
6.91
), (
6.92
) and (
6.93
). In the same way, we also obtain the specific constant of the estimate
of (
6.86
)
which will be convenient in our scheme.

We finally turn to (
6.87
) and (
6.88
).
Let us first note that revisiting (
6.75
), with a little bit of averaging, we have the bounds

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\tau_{0}+L)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)d\tau+\left(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)d\tau}\sqrt{\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)d\tau}+\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{4}},

where we have used also the interpolation inequality (
3.115
) (and that
L\geq\frac{1}{2}
).

We now notice that the above bound clearly holds also for

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}^{\prime}:={}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\tau_{0}+L)+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)d\tau
,
For (
6.87
), we revisit (
6.72
), noting that we may replace

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}
by
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}^{\prime}
on the right hand side,
and thus we may bound

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+L]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+AC\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}^{\prime}+C\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+C\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}+AC(\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{4}})+C\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}L^{(-1+\delta)/2}+C\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{12}L^{-\frac{3}{2}+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}+\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}L^{-\frac{1}{4}},

and we have used a higher power of
\alpha

to absorb constants.

Finally, for (
6.88
), we revisit (
6.75
) for
k-2
replacing
k-1
,
to obtain

\displaystyle{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\left(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L^{-1}

whence we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+L]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})+A\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+C\,\left({}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\right)+C\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}+A\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-1}+C\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}L^{(-2+\delta)/2}+C\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}L^{(-2+\delta)/2}L^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}+\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}L^{-\frac{1}{4}},

where we have again used a higher power of
\alpha
to absorb all other constants.
∎

6.3.4
The iteration: proof of Theorem
5.1
in case (iii)

We may now prove Theorem
5.1
in case (iii).

As in case (ii), we shall proceed iteratively. The proof is a simple modification of
that of Section
6.2.4
.

We define
\tau_{0}=1
,
L_{0}=1
,
L_{i}=2^{i}
,
\tau_{i+1}=\tau_{i}+L_{i}
,
and fix
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
so that the statement of Proposition
6.3.3
holds,
for instance
\alpha:=\alpha_{0}
. Define the parameter

d:=\Pi_{i=1}^{\infty}(1+2^{-\frac{i}{4}}\alpha)<\infty.

(6.108)

(Note that we shall no longer note the dependence of constants and parameters on
\alpha
since it is now considered fixed.
Thus implicit constants depending on the choice of
\alpha
will from now on be incorporated in the notations
\sim
and
\lesssim
.)

For
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
sufficiently small,
since

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(6.109)

then in view of Corollary
4.5.3
, we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

Thus, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\rm global}\ll\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}
,
and all
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}
, then if (
6.109
) is satisfied,
it
follows that there exists a
\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}(\varepsilon_{0})\sim\varepsilon_{0}
,
satisfying

\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{d}\varepsilon_{\rm slab},\qquad\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{d}\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}

(6.110)

and

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L_{0}^{-1},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha L_{0}^{-1+\delta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}L_{0}^{-2+\delta}.

Finally, with our restriction on the definition of
\varepsilon_{0}
, we may also write

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}.

In general, given

\tau_{i}\geq 1
defined above,
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}
, and a solution
\psi

of (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})

such that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{i},

(6.111)

and

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha L_{i}^{-1},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha L_{i}^{-1+\delta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{-2+\delta},

(6.112)

note that by our restrictions on
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}
, the assumptions of Proposition
6.2.3

hold with
\alpha^{2}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}
in place of
\varepsilon_{0}
(here we are using (
6.111
) to invoke Corollary
4.5.3

to rewrite (
6.60
) in terms of the calligraphic energies; cf. the
first lines of the proof of Proposition
6.3.3
) and the assumptions of
Proposition
6.3.3
then apply with
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}
in place of
\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}
,
where both propositions are understood now with

\tau_{i}
,
\tau_{i+1}
in place of
\tau_{0}
,
\tau_{1}
.
It follows that the solution
\psi
extends to a solution defined also in

\mathcal{R}_{i}:=\mathcal{R}(\tau_{i},\tau_{i}+L_{i})
satisfying the estimates
(
6.86
)–(
6.89
) while for
\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{i+1}=\tau_{i}+L_{i}

we have in addition (
6.92
)–(
6.93
).
We have thus

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\alpha,

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}(1+\alpha L_{i}^{-\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}(1+\alpha L_{i}^{-\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha L_{i}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\alpha L_{i+1}^{-1},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha L_{i}^{-1+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\alpha L_{i+1}^{-1+\delta},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau_{i+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}\alpha^{2}L_{i}^{-2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\alpha^{2}L_{i+1}^{-2+\delta},

as long as

\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}:=\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}(1+\alpha L_{i}^{-\frac{1}{4}}).

(6.113)

By our requirement (
6.110
) and the
definition (
6.108
) of the parameter
d
, then defining

\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}
inductively by (
6.113
), it follows
that
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}
and
\alpha\hat{\varepsilon}_{i+1}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm slab}
.

It follows that a solution exists in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)=\cup\mathcal{R}(\tau_{i},\tau_{i}+L_{i})

and in each interval the estimates (
6.89
)–(
6.90
) hold,
with
L=L_{i}
.

We obtain finally that for all
\tau\geq 1
we have (among other estimates)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},

(6.114)

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\log(\tau+1),

(6.115)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},

(6.116)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-1+\delta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-1+\delta},

(6.117)

\int_{\tau}^{\infty}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu5$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-1+\delta},

(6.118)

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-2+\delta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{F}}}(v,\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-2+\delta},

(6.119)

\int_{\tau}^{\infty}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu8$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\tau^{-2+\delta}.

(6.120)

Finally, let us note that we may improve (
6.115
)
to

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.121)

This follows, for all
\tau
, by applying
again both estimates of Proposition
4.8.1
appropriate to case (iii)
globally in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
.
One may now re-estimate all nonlinear spacetime integrals arising in the estimates on dyadic intervals and sum.

Appendix A
The physical space identity on very slowly rotating Kerr

In this appendix, we show that very slowly rotating Kerr metrics indeed satisfy the assumptions
of Section
3.4.3
.

Theorem A.1
.

Consider the manifold
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
of
Section
2.7.3
, where
g_{a,M}
denotes the Kerr metric and

|a|\ll M
.
Then there exist currents
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
,
K^{V,w,q}
associated to
the wave operator
\Box_{g_{a,M}}
, satisfying the assumptions of Section
3.4.3
.

Theorem
A.1

actually holds for general suitably small stationary perturbations of Schwarzschild satisfying the assumptions of Section
2

and appropriate decay at infinity. So as not to complicate matters more, here we will simply do explicitly the computation for slowly rotating Kerr
|a|\ll M
.

The nontrivial computations necessary to produce
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
and
K^{V,w,q}

all involve the exterior region. As a result, we may work entirely in Boyer–Lindquist coordinates.
Thus,
in this appendix,
r
will denote the Boyer–Lindquist coordinate, and
not
the function (
2.1
) of Section
2.1
.
We will show explicitly the positivity
properties in the region
r>r_{+}
, which can be covered globally by such Boyer–Lindquist coordinates.
The extension of the coercivity properties to the manifold of Section
2.7.3
follows softly, without computation.
(See already the end of Section
A.4
).

Our currents will be explicit, but rather than give formulae for the final
(V,w,q,\varpi)
, we will build the current from its natural constituent pieces.
In particular, so as to directly generate positive
0
’th order terms in the boundary currents, we will make use of the twisted energy momentum tensor
as defined in
[
HW14
]
.

This appendix is organised as follows.
We first recall the Kerr metric in Boyer–Lindquist coordinates in Section
A.1
.
We shall then review in Section
A.2
the twisted energy momentum tensor and its basic properties.
In the next two sections we shall build our current as a combination of a “Morawetz current”, constructed
in Section
A.3
(vanishing, however, identically in an open set contained trapped null geodesics), and the twisted
stationary current and redshift currents, constructed in Section
A.4
. Section
A.5
contains some auxiliary calculations.

A.1
The Kerr metric in Boyer–Lindquist coordinates

We define

\displaystyle\Delta=r^{2}-2Mr+a^{2}\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \Sigma=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\,.

(A.1)

We recall the
(t,r,\theta,\phi)
Boyer–Lindquist coordinates and the associated
(t,r^{\star},\theta,\phi)

coordinates (which we will also refer to as Boyer–Lindquist coordinates) with the familiar relation

\frac{dr^{\star}}{dr}=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\,.

We shall consider these in the domain
\mathbb{R}\times(r_{+},\infty)\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, where
(\theta,\phi)
are identified with the usual
spherical coordinates of
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
The Kerr metric then has the form

g=g_{a,M}=g_{tt}dt\otimes dt+g_{t\phi}(dt\otimes d\phi+d\phi\otimes dt)+g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}dr^{\star}\otimes dr^{\star}+g_{\theta\theta}d\theta\otimes d\theta+g_{\phi\phi}d\phi\otimes d\phi\,,

with

\displaystyle g_{tt}

\displaystyle=-\left(1-\frac{2Mr}{\Sigma}\right)\ \ \ ,\ \ \ g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}=\frac{\Sigma\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\ \ \ ,\ \ \ g_{\theta\theta}=\Sigma\,,

\displaystyle g_{t\phi}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2Mr}{\Sigma}a\sin^{2}\theta\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \ \ g_{\phi\phi}=\left(r^{2}+a^{2}+\frac{2Mra^{2}}{\Sigma}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\sin^{2}\theta\,.

(A.2)

We compute the inverse metric components as

\displaystyle g^{tt}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\Delta}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}+\frac{2Mra^{2}}{\Sigma}\sin^{2}\theta\right)\ \ \ ,\ \ \ g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}=\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Sigma\Delta}\ \ \ ,\ \ \ g^{\theta\theta}=\frac{1}{\Sigma}\,,

\displaystyle g^{t\phi}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2Mr}{\Sigma\Delta}a\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \ \ g^{\phi\phi}=\frac{\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Sigma\Delta\sin^{2}\theta}\,.

(A.3)

For the determinant in these coordinates we note

\displaystyle\sqrt{|g|}=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Sigma\sin\theta=g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\right)\sin\theta\,.

(A.4)

A.2
The twisted energy momentum tensor

We consider the covariant wave equation
\Box_{g}\psi=g^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\psi=0
for
g=g_{a,M}
the Kerr metric.
Note that this equation can be rewritten as follows:
With the function
\beta=\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{-1}
, we
define as in
[
HW14
]
the twisted operators

\displaystyle\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}(\cdot)=\beta\nabla_{\mu}\left(\beta^{-1}\cdot\right)\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \tilde{\nabla}^{\dagger}_{\mu}(\cdot)=-\beta^{-1}\nabla_{\mu}\left(\beta\cdot\right)

(A.5)

and rewrite the wave equation as

\displaystyle 0=\Box_{g}\psi

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}^{\dagger}\tilde{\nabla}^{\mu}\psi-\mathcal{V}\psi=0

\displaystyle=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\nabla_{\mu}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\nabla^{\mu}\left(\psi\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)-\mathcal{V}\psi\,

(A.6)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Box_{g}\beta}{\beta}=-\frac{1}{g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}(r^{2}+a^{2})\beta}\partial_{r^{\star}}\left(\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\right)\partial_{r^{\star}}\beta\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Sigma}\cdot\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r(r-4M)+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}=:\frac{1}{\Sigma}\mathcal{V}_{0}\,.

(A.7)

We now define the twisted energy momentum tensor

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\left(\tilde{\nabla}^{\alpha}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\alpha}\psi+\mathcal{V}\psi^{2}\right)

(A.8)

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\left[{\nabla}_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi){\nabla}_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\left({\nabla}^{\alpha}(\beta^{-1}\psi){\nabla}_{\alpha}(\beta^{-1}\psi)+\mathcal{V}\beta^{-2}\psi^{2}\right)\right].

We note that for all
|a|<M
,
\mathcal{V}
is strictly positive in the exterior, in fact

\mathcal{V}\geq\frac{2Mr(r-M)(r+M)}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\gtrsim r^{-3},

(A.9)

since
r\geq r_{+}>M
,
and
\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]
satisfies the dominant energy condition,
i.e.
\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]\xi^{\mu}\xi^{\nu}
is nonnegative if
\xi
is timelike,
and in fact controls coercively first derivatives of
\psi
as well as
\psi
itself (the latter with the weight
r^{-3}
).
(Indeed, this direct control of the zero’th order term is our motivation for considering (
A.8
) in place of the usual
T_{\mu\nu}
.).

From Proposition 3 of
[
HW14
]
we infer:

Proposition A.2.1
.

For
\psi
a
C^{2}
solution of
\Box_{g}\psi=0
and
X
a smooth spacetime vector field we have the identity

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]=\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]\,,

(A.10)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu}\,,

(A.11)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]

\displaystyle={}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]+X^{\nu}\tilde{S}_{\nu}[\psi]\,

(A.12)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{\mu}[\psi]=\frac{\tilde{\nabla}^{\dagger}_{\mu}(\beta\mathcal{V})}{2\beta}\psi^{2}+\frac{\tilde{\nabla}^{\dagger}_{\mu}\beta}{2\beta}\tilde{\nabla}^{\nu}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi\,.

(A.13)

We also have the Lagrangian identity for an arbitrary spacetime function
w
:

Proposition A.2.2
.

For
\psi
a
C^{2}
solution of
\Box_{g}\psi=0
and
w
a smooth spacetime function we have the identity

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\tilde{J}_{\mu}^{aux,w}=\tilde{K}^{aux,w}[\psi]

(A.14)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{aux,w}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=w\beta^{2}\nabla^{\alpha}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\nabla_{\alpha}(\beta^{-1}\psi)+(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{\mu}\left(\beta^{2}\nabla_{\mu}w\right)+\mathcal{V}w\beta^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{aux,w}_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=w\beta^{2}\left(\beta^{-1}\psi\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\psi^{2}\nabla_{\mu}w.

Proof.

This follows from

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\left(w\left(\psi\beta^{-1}\beta^{2}\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)\right)=

\displaystyle w\beta^{2}\nabla^{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\nabla^{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)+w\psi\left(-\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}^{\dagger}\tilde{\nabla}^{\mu}\psi\right)

\displaystyle+(\nabla^{\mu}w)\beta^{2}\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}

(A.15)

after rearranging and inserting (
A.2
).
∎

Remark A.2.3
.

Note that when
w
is a function of
r
only (as will always be the case in the applications below) we have

\nabla^{\mu}\left(\beta^{2}\nabla_{\mu}w\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\left(\sqrt{g}g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\beta^{2}\partial_{r^{\star}}w\right)=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta\Sigma}\partial_{r^{\star}}^{2}w\,,

a formula which is useful in the computations below.

A.3
A Morawetz current vanishing identically in a neighbourhood of trapping

In this section, we shall produce a current giving the desired bulk positivity properties modulo suitable zero’th order terms, but with additional degeneration
at the horizon, and without the boundary positivity properties. (The horizon degeneration and the boundary positivity properties
will be dealt with in Section
A.4
.)

The point about this current is that it will completely vanish in the set
11M/4\leq r\leq 7M/2
, which contains all trapped null geodesics for
|a|\ll M
.
Thus,
this current is insensitive to the precise nature of the dynamics near trapping
. The requirement of vanishing on such a set will necessarily generate
lower order terms, however, with an unfavourable sign. These too will be supported away from trapping.

Our current will be defined combining those of Proposition
A.2.1
and
A.2.2
, and the
vector field component of the current will be in the direction of
\partial_{r^{\star}}
. We begin with a computation (note that
′
below will denote
\frac{d}{dr^{*}}
):

Lemma A.3.1
.

For
\beta^{-1}=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}
,
h=\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
and
X=f(r^{*})\partial_{r^{\star}}
(with
f
arbitrary) we have for any

\updelta\in\mathbb{R}
the divergence identity

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\left(\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}_{\mu}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{J}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{K}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]\,,

(A.16)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]

\displaystyle+\tilde{J}_{\mu}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\tilde{J}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu}+f^{\prime}\beta^{2}\left(\beta^{-1}\psi\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\psi^{2}\nabla_{\mu}f^{\prime}\,

\displaystyle\qquad-\updelta\left[f\cdot h\beta^{2}\left(\beta^{-1}\psi\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\psi^{2}\nabla_{\mu}f\cdot h\right]

(A.17)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{f\partial_{r^{\star}}}[\psi]

\displaystyle+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\tilde{K}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}\left(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f-\updelta fh\right)\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}f\beta^{2}\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\mu\nu}-2\updelta hg^{\mu\nu}\right)\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta\Sigma}\Bigg(-\frac{1}{4}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f\partial_{r^{\star}}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathcal{V}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\updelta\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(f\cdot h\right)^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{V}_{0}f\cdot h\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right]\Bigg)(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}

(A.18)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mu\nu}=-\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})+g^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\,.

(A.19)

Proof.

We compute (see Section
A.5
)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{f\partial_{r^{\star}}}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}f\beta^{2}\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\left[\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-g^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\right]\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\beta^{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f\right)g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}(q^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\beta}(\beta^{-1}\psi)-\frac{1}{2}f\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{2}\mathcal{V})(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}\right)\mathcal{V}\psi^{2}.

(A.20)

Adding the Lagrangian identity of Proposition
A.2.2
with
w=\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}
(and using Remark
A.2.3
) we deduce the result for
\updelta=0
. For arbitrary
\updelta
we simply add the Lagrangian identity of Proposition
A.2.2
with
w=\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})}f=f\cdot h
and group terms.
∎

We now exploit the divergence identity of Lemma
A.3.1
. For this we first define the following (disjoint) decomposition of the range of the
r
-variable:

\displaystyle[r_{+},\infty)

\displaystyle=R_{1}\cup R_{2}\cup R_{3}\cup R_{4}\cup R_{5}

\displaystyle=\left[r_{+},\frac{5}{2}M\right)\cup\left[\frac{5}{2}M,\frac{11}{4}M\right)\cup\left[\frac{11}{4}M,\frac{7}{2}M\right)\cup\left[\frac{7}{2}M,4M\right)\cup\left[4M,\infty\right).

Note that
\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\in R_{3}\}
includes the region containing all trapped null geodesics if
\frac{a}{M}
is suitably small.

Proposition A.3.2
.

There exists an (explicit) function
f
such that for all
\frac{|a|}{M}
sufficiently small we can choose
\updelta>0
sufficiently small (depending only on
M
) such that the estimate

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{f\partial_{r^{\star}}}[\psi]+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{f\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}}_{\mu}[\psi]\geq

\displaystyle\qquad c\mathbbm{1}_{R_{1}\cup R_{5}}\left(\frac{\left(\partial_{t}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}}{r^{4}}+\frac{1}{r^{5}}|\mathring{\nabla}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-C\mathbbm{1}_{R_{2}\cup R_{4}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}\,

(A.21)

holds for constants
c
and
C
depending only on
M
. Here we have defined the shorthand
|\mathring{\nabla}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}:=\left(\partial_{\theta}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\left(\partial_{\phi}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}
.

Proof.

In the proof, we let
\tilde{c}
and
\tilde{C}
be constants depending only on
M
(but which might change from line to line).
Starting from (
A.3.1
) we define
\mathcal{B}^{\mu\nu}=\mathcal{A}^{\mu\nu}-2\updelta\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}g^{\mu\nu}
and compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{tt}

\displaystyle=2a^{2}\cdot\frac{r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta+2\updelta\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}+\frac{2Mra^{2}}{\Sigma}\sin^{2}\theta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{t\phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2aM(a^{2}-3r^{2})}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+2\updelta\cdot a\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{2Mr}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\phi\phi}

\displaystyle=2\frac{r^{2}(r-3M)+a^{2}r\cos[2\theta]+a^{2}M}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}-2\updelta\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\sin^{2}\theta},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\theta\theta}

\displaystyle=2\frac{r^{2}(r-3M)+a^{2}r+a^{2}M}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}-2\updelta\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta}{\Sigma(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\,.

(A.22)

From this one sees that for
\frac{|a|}{M}
sufficiently small we can choose
\updelta
sufficiently small (depending only on
M
), such that in
\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\notin R_{3}\}
(where
|1-\frac{3M}{r}|\geq\frac{1}{12}
) the estimates

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{B}^{tt}}{(1-\frac{3M}{r})}\geq\frac{\tilde{c}}{r^{2}}\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \frac{\mathcal{B}^{\phi\phi}}{(1-\frac{3M}{r})}\geq\frac{\tilde{c}}{r^{3}\sin^{2}\theta}\ \ \ ,\ \ \ \frac{\mathcal{B}^{\theta\theta}}{(1-\frac{3M}{r})}\geq\frac{\tilde{c}}{r^{3}}

(A.23)

hold for a constant
\tilde{c}
depending only on
M
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathcal{B}^{t\phi}}{(1-\frac{3M}{r})}\Big|\leq\frac{|a|}{M}\frac{\tilde{C}}{r^{4}}

(A.24)

We next choose
f:\left[r_{+},\infty\right)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
to be bounded, monotonically increasing as follows

f=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}-\frac{M}{r}&\text{if }r\in R_{1},\\
\textrm{$C^{3}$ interpolate}&\text{if }r\in R_{2},\\
0&\text{if }r\in R_{3},\\
\textrm{$C^{3}$ interpolate}&\text{if }r\in R_{4},\\
1-\frac{M}{r}&\text{if }r\in R_{5}.\end{array}\right.

Specifically, we do the
C^{3}
interpolation such that we have
f
monotonically increasing and

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\geq\tilde{c}(-f)\ \ \ \ \textrm{ in $R_{2}$}\ \ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \ f^{\prime}\geq\tilde{c}f\ \ \ \ \textrm{ in $R_{4}$}

(A.25)

for a fixed constant
\tilde{c}>0
depending only on
M
. In particular (potentially making
\updelta
slightly smaller), we can achieve that

g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f-\updelta f\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\geq f\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\frac{\tilde{c}}{r^{2}}

holds in all of
\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\notin R_{3}\}
for a
\tilde{c}
depending only on
M
. Since
f\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)
is globally non-negative and in fact bounded uniformly below by a
\tilde{c}
in
R_{1}\cup R_{5}
, the estimate (
A.3.2
) now follows except for the zeroth order term on the right hand side. (For this the only thing to notice is that in

\frac{1}{2}f\beta^{2}\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)\left[\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\frac{\mathcal{B}^{\mu\nu}}{1-\frac{3M}{r}}\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right]

the quadratic form in the square bracket is positive definite by the estimates (
A.23
) and (
A.24
).)

For the zeroth order term, clearly we only need to establish a lower bound in
R_{1}\cup R_{5}
.
We start with
R_{5}
, where we have for
f=1-\frac{M}{r}
that

-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-f\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}V_{0}\right)^{\prime}=\frac{3M}{r^{4}}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-5})

and

\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r^{2}-2Mr+a^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-f\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathcal{V}_{0}\right)^{\prime}\right)=\frac{M}{r^{4}}\left(3-\frac{2M}{r}-14\frac{M^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)\ \ \ \textrm{if $a=0$.}

Since the left hand side of the second identity is continuous in
a
and the bracket on the right hand side uniformly bounded below by
\frac{1}{3}
for
r\geq 3M
, the estimate claimed for the zeroth order term follows for sufficiently small
\frac{|a|}{M}
after potentially making
\updelta
smaller (note
\updelta\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r^{2}-2Mr+a^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(f\cdot h\right)^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{V}_{0}f\cdot h\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{\tilde{C}\updelta}{r^{4}})
.

Similarly, in
R_{1}
we have for
f=-\frac{M}{r}
the identity

\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{r^{2}-2Mr+a^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-f(w\mathcal{V}_{0})^{\prime}\right)=-\frac{M}{r^{6}}\left(14M^{2}-14Mr+3r^{2}\right)\ \ \ \textrm{if $a=0$.}

The expression on the right hand side is easily shown to be uniformly bounded below by
\frac{M^{3}}{4r^{6}}
for
r\in[9M/5,11M/4]
. Since the expression on the left is in particular continuous in
a
on
[r_{+},11M/4]
the estimate claimed for the zeroth order term also follows in
R_{1}
.
∎

A.4
Adding the redshift and stationary currents and completing the proof

Let
T
denote the stationary Killing field of Kerr (which in Boyer–Lindquist coordinates is
T=\partial_{t}
) and
N
the timelike (including on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
) redshift vector
field constructed in Theorem 7.1 of
[
DR13
]
. We have that
\tilde{K}^{T}=0
while

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{N}[\psi]\geq\tilde{c}\Big(

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+|\mathring{\nabla}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}+(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\partial_{t}-\partial_{r^{\star}}+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}\right)(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right]^{2}\Big)\ \ \textrm{in $r\leq\frac{9}{4}M$}

provided
a
is sufficiently small. We also have
N=T
in the complement of
R_{1}
.

We shall now complete the proof of Theorem
A.1
by adding
\Upsilon\cdot\tilde{J}^{T}_{\mu}[\psi]+\eta\tilde{J}^{N}_{\mu}[\psi]
to the current
of Proposition
A.3.2
, for constants
\Upsilon>0
(large) and
\eta>0
(small).

We consider the divergence identity of Lemma
A.3.1
with
\updelta
and
f
now chosen as in Proposition
A.3.2
.
We expand the identity as follows for constants
\Upsilon>0
(large) and
\eta>0
(small) to be chosen below as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\left(\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}_{\mu}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{J}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]+\Upsilon\cdot\tilde{J}^{T}_{\mu}[\psi]+\eta\cdot\tilde{J}^{N}_{\mu}[\psi]\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{K}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]+\eta\cdot\tilde{K}^{N}[\psi]\,.

(A.26)

It is now clear that we can choose
\eta
sufficiently small (depending only on
M
) such that for
r\geq\frac{9}{4}M
we can absorb the term
\eta\tilde{K}^{N}[\psi]
, which is supported only in
R_{1}
, by the positivity of
\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\cdot\tilde{K}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]
established in Proposition
A.3.2
to deduce

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{f\partial_{r^{\star}}}[\psi]+\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}[\psi]-\updelta\tilde{K}^{aux,\frac{f\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}}_{\mu}[\psi]+\eta\tilde{K}^{N}[\psi]\geq

(A.27)

\displaystyle+c\mathbbm{1}_{R_{1}\cup R_{5}}\left(\frac{\left(\partial_{t}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}+(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}}{r^{4}}+\frac{1}{r^{5}}|\mathring{\nabla}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+c\mathbbm{1}_{R_{1}}\left[\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}\left(\partial_{t}-\partial_{r^{\star}}+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}\right)(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right]^{2}-C\mathbbm{1}_{R_{2}\cup R_{4}}|(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}\,,

where
c
may be smaller than in (
A.3.2
) but still depends only on
M
. We finally claim that we can choose
\Upsilon
sufficiently large in (
A.4
) such that

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{aux,\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime}}_{\mu}[\psi]-\updelta\tilde{J}^{aux,f\cdot h}_{\mu}[\psi]+\Upsilon\tilde{J}^{T}_{\mu}[\psi]+\eta\tilde{J}^{N}_{\mu}[\psi]\right)n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\boxed{\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]\left(X^{\nu}+\Upsilon T^{\nu}+\eta N^{\nu}\right)n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}}+f^{\prime}\beta^{2}\left(\beta^{-1}\psi\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\ \ \ -\frac{1}{2}\psi^{2}\nabla_{\mu}f^{\prime}n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}-\updelta\left[f\cdot h\beta^{2}\left(\beta^{-1}\psi\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\psi^{2}\nabla_{\mu}f\cdot h\right]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\geq\beta^{2}\left(|L(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}|\underline{L}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}|\mathring{\nabla}(\beta^{-1}\psi)|^{2}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}\right).

(A.28)

For the boxed term, the estimate is an immediate consequence of the
twisted energy momentum tensor (
A.8
) satisfying the dominant energy condition, as discussed in Section
A.2
,
the positivity (
A.9
),
and the fact that the vector field
\Upsilon T+\eta N+X
is timelike for sufficiently large
\Upsilon
, provided that we then restrict to
|a|
sufficiently small.
Moreover, for the boxed term the zeroth order term in the estimate scales with
\Upsilon
, i.e. the larger we choose
\Upsilon
the larger the zeroth order term becomes. This can be used to absorb the remaining terms using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, for
|a|
sufficiently small depending on this final choice of
\Upsilon
.

The relations (
A.27
) and (
A.28
) give the coercivity properties (
3.31
) and the first of (
3.32
),
restricted to the exterior region,
where we define
\rho=\mathbbm{1}_{R_{1}\cup R_{5}}
and
\xi=\mathbbm{1}_{R_{2}\cup R_{4}}
,
except that the
r
decay for the bulk current is not optimised to the
r^{-1-\delta}
and
r^{-3-\delta}
weights for the first and zeroth order terms, respectively, and the
r
decay for the boundary current is not optimised to the
r^{-2}
weight for the zeroth order term. (Note
that the Boyer–Lindquist
r
and the
r
of (
2.1
) are comparable for large
r
values so one can compare directly
the
r
-decay as if the coordinates were the same.) To remedy this, it suffices to add
\epsilon J^{\hat{\chi}V,\hat{\chi}w,\hat{\chi}q,\hat{\chi}\varpi}
,

\epsilon K^{\hat{\chi}V,\hat{\chi}w,\hat{\chi}q}
, respectively to the currents, where
(V,w,q,\varpi)
here denotes the current of Appendix
B.1
,

\hat{\chi}
is a cutoff supported far away, and
\epsilon
is sufficiently small. The resulting currents now indeed satisfy (
3.31
) and the first inequality of (
3.32
), restricted to the exterior, with weights as stated. We note finally that for small
|a|
, we may take the
\chi
in
the estimate (
3.3
) proven
in
[
DR10
]
or
[
DRSR16
]
to be identically one outside of a small neighbourhood of
r=3M
. Thus, our
\xi
indeed satisfies (
3.27
).

The currents trivially may be extended to the slightly larger bigger domain of Section
2.7.3
. Note finally that
the additional positivity statements of (
3.32
) are easily shown to hold.
Rewriting the current in terms of a single quadruple
(V,w,q,\varpi)
, one easily sees that the boundedness statements (
3.16
) hold as well.
This completes the proof of Theorem
A.1
.

A.5
Computation of the
X
-deformation tensor

We collect here some computations which were used in the proof of Theorem
A.1
.

We have

\displaystyle-2{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}=X^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}(g^{\mu\nu})-\partial_{\alpha}X^{\mu}g^{\alpha\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}X^{\nu}g^{\alpha\mu}\,,

(A.29)

hence for
X=f(r)\partial_{r^{\star}}

\displaystyle-2{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}=f\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-\partial_{\alpha}X^{\mu}g^{\alpha\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}X^{\nu}g^{\alpha\mu}\,,

(A.30)

which we can write (using
g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}=1
for Kerr in Boyer-Lindquist) as

\displaystyle-2{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=f\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})+fg^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})-fg^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\ \ \ \textrm{unless $\mu=\nu=r^{\star}$}

\displaystyle-2{}^{(X)}\pi^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}

\displaystyle=fg^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})-2g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f,

(A.31)

so

-2{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}=g^{\mu\nu}\cdot fg_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})+\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}-2g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f&\textrm{if $\mu=\nu=r^{\star}$}\\
f\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-fg^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})&\textrm{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

We note also

\displaystyle tr{}^{(X)}\pi=g_{\mu\nu}{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}f\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})+\partial_{r^{\star}}f=f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}+\partial_{r^{\star}}f.

(A.32)

Therefore

\displaystyle{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi-\frac{1}{2}(tr{}^{(X)}\pi)g^{\mu\nu}\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi-\frac{1}{2}(tr{}^{(X)}\pi)\mathcal{V}\psi^{2}

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\beta^{2}f\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\left[\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-g^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\right]\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}+fg_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}})^{-1}\right)g^{\mu\nu}\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}\right)\mathcal{V}\psi^{2}.

(A.33)

Noting that the determinant
\sqrt{g}=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Sigma\sin\theta=g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\right)\sin\theta
for Kerr in Boyer–Lindquist
(t,r^{\star},\theta,\phi)
,

\displaystyle{}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\beta^{2}f\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\left[\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-g^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\right]\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{2r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\psi\partial_{\nu}\psi-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}\right)\mathcal{V}\psi^{2}.

We now recall from Proposition
A.2.1
the definition
\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]={}^{(X)}\pi^{\mu\nu}\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]+X^{\nu}\tilde{S}_{\nu}[\psi]
and compute

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{X}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}f\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}f\beta^{2}\sum_{\mu,\nu\neq r^{\star}}\left[\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{\mu\nu})-g^{\mu\nu}g_{r^{\star}r^{\star}}\partial_{r^{\star}}(g^{r^{\star}r^{\star}})\right]\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\beta^{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{2r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+f\frac{\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{2})}{\beta^{2}}\right)g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)\partial_{\nu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}f\partial_{r^{\star}}(\beta^{2}\mathcal{V})(\beta^{-1}\psi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{r^{\star}}f+f\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\partial_{r^{\star}}\sqrt{g}\right)\mathcal{V}\psi^{2},

(A.34)

which indeed simplifies to (
A.3
).

Appendix B
Energy currents in Minkowski space

In this section the energy currents used to obtain the assumptions of Section
3.4.1
and Section
3.5
are described in Minkowski space. In Section
B.1
a quadruple
(V,w,q,\varpi)
satisfying the assumptions of Section
3.4.1
is introduced, and in
Section
B.2
a quadruple
(\tilde{V}_{\rm far},\tilde{w}_{\rm far},\tilde{q}_{\rm far},\tilde{\varpi}_{\rm far})
satisfying the assumptions of
Section
3.5
is introduced.

Recall that, for a given spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g)
and suitably regular function
\psi\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}
, for a given vector field
V
, a function
w
, a one form
q
and a two form
\varpi
, the energy current
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
takes the form

J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[g,\psi]:=T_{\mu\nu}[g,\psi]V^{\nu}+w\psi\partial_{\mu}\psi+\psi^{2}q_{\mu}+*d\big(\psi^{2}\varpi\big)_{\mu}.

Here, for
0\leq k\leq 4
,
*\colon\Lambda^{k}\mathcal{M}\to\Lambda^{4-k}\mathcal{M}
denotes the Hodge star operator which satisfies, for all
\alpha,\beta\in\Lambda^{k}\mathcal{M}
,

\alpha\wedge*\beta=g(\alpha,\beta)d\mathrm{Vol}_{\mathcal{M}}.

The divergence of
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
takes the form

\nabla^{\mu}J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[g,\psi]=K^{V,w,q}[g,\psi]+H^{V,w}[\psi]\Box_{g}\psi,

where

\displaystyle K^{V,w,q}[g,\psi]

\displaystyle:=\pi^{V}_{\mu\nu}[g]T^{\mu\nu}[g,\psi]+\psi\nabla^{\mu}w\nabla_{\mu}\psi+w\nabla^{\mu}\psi\nabla_{\mu}\psi+\psi^{2}\nabla^{\mu}q_{\mu}+2\psi g^{\mu\nu}q_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\psi,

\displaystyle H^{V,w}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=V^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\psi+w\psi.

The divergence of a one form
\xi\in\Lambda^{1}\mathcal{M}
can be expressed in terms of
d
and
*
by

\mathrm{Div}\xi=*\,d*\xi.

In particular it follows that, for any two form
\varpi\in\Lambda^{2}\mathcal{M}
and any function
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
,

\mathrm{Div}*d\big(\psi^{2}\varpi\big)=-*dd\big(\psi^{2}\varpi\big)=0.

Thus the choice of
\varpi
in the current
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[g,\psi]
never contributes to the associated
K^{V,w,q}[g,\psi]
or
H^{V,w}[g,\psi]
. Moreover
q
does not contribute to
H^{V,w}[g,\psi]
.

Throughout this section
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
denotes Minkowski space (see Section
2.7.1
),
and

r=\sqrt{(x^{1})^{2}+(x^{2})^{2}+(x^{3})^{2}}

(B.1)

denotes the standard radial coordinate and
not

the
r
of (
2.1
).
(Note that (
B.1
) was denoted
\tilde{r}
in Section
2.7.1
. Since (
2.1
) and (
B.1
)
are comparable for large
r
, the associated
r
-weighted coercivity properties will be equivalent.)
Recall the
(t,r,\vartheta,\varphi)
and the
(u,v,\vartheta,\varphi)
coordinate systems. The Minkowski metric takes the form

g_{0}=-dt^{2}+dr^{2}+r^{2}(d\vartheta^{2}+\sin^{2}\vartheta d\varphi^{2})=-dudv+r^{2}(d\vartheta^{2}+\sin^{2}\vartheta d\varphi^{2}).

The following basic properties of Minkowski space are used. The spacetime volume form of Minkowski space can be written

d\mathrm{Vol}_{\mathcal{M}}=r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ dt\wedge dr\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi=\frac{1}{2}r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ du\wedge dv\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi,

and so the Hodge star operator
*
in particular satisfies

*\left(r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ dr\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi\right)=-dt,\qquad*\left(r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ dt\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi\right)=-dr,

(B.2)

*\left(r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ du\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi\right)=du,\qquad*\left(r^{2}\sin\vartheta\ dv\wedge d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi\right)=-dv.

(B.3)

The normals to the hypersurfaces
\Sigma(\tau)
and
\underline{C}_{v}
take the form

n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}=\partial_{v}+\iota_{r\leq R}\partial_{u},\qquad n_{\underline{C}_{v}}=\partial_{u}.

(B.4)

B.1
The
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
current

In the case that
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
is Minkowski space, the tuple
(V,w,q,\varpi)
of Section
3.4.1
can be defined as follows.
Consider

V_{1}=T=\partial_{u}+\partial_{v},\qquad w_{1}=0,\qquad q_{1}=0,\qquad\varpi_{1}=0,

V_{2}=\delta_{1}T=\delta_{1}(\partial_{u}+\partial_{v}),\qquad w_{2}=0,\qquad q_{2}=0,\qquad\varpi_{2}=-\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}r^{-1}r^{2}\sin\vartheta d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi,

V_{3}=\frac{\delta_{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{3}}{(1+r)^{\delta}}\right)(\partial_{v}-\partial_{u}),\qquad w_{3}=\frac{\delta_{2}}{r}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{3}}{(1+r)^{\delta}}\right),\qquad(q_{3})_{\mu}=-\frac{\partial_{x^{\mu}}w_{3}}{2},\qquad\varpi_{3}=0,

for appropriate

0<\delta_{3}\ll\delta_{2}\ll\delta_{1}\ll 1,

(B.5)

and define

V=\sum_{i=1}^{3}V_{i},\qquad w=\sum_{i=1}^{3}w_{i},\qquad q=\sum_{i=1}^{3}q_{i},\qquad\varpi=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varpi_{i},

(B.6)

and then, for a given
\psi
, define currents
J^{V_{i},w_{i},q_{i},\varpi_{i}}_{\mu}[\psi]
and
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[\psi]
by (
3.11
).
These satisfy the boundedness properties (
3.16
).
(Note that the current
J^{V_{2},w_{2},q_{2},\alpha_{2}}_{\mu}[g_{0},\psi]
can be viewed as arising from contracting the twisted energy momentum tensor, defined in (
A.8
), with the Killing vector field
\delta_{1}T
.)

Proposition B.1.1

(The
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}
current in Minkowski space)
.

With
(V,w,q,\varpi)
defined as above, if
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
and
\delta_{3}
are chosen according to (
B.5
), the current
J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[\psi]
satisfies the coercivity relations (
3.18
) and the corresponding
K^{V,w,q}[\psi]
satisfies the coercivity relation (
3.17
). More precisely,

\displaystyle J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\gtrsim(\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}\left((\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\partial_{u}(r\psi)|^{2}\right)+(1+r)^{-2}\psi^{2},

(B.7)

\displaystyle J^{V,w,q,\varpi}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\gtrsim(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+r^{-2}|\partial_{u}(r\psi)|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(1+r)^{-2}\psi^{2},

(B.8)

\displaystyle K^{V,w,q}[\psi]

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{-1}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(1+r)^{-1-\delta}\left((\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+(\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}\right)+(1+r)^{-3-\delta}\psi^{2}.

(B.9)

Moreover the corresponding
H^{V,w}[\psi]
satisfies

|H^{V,w}[\psi]|\lesssim|\partial_{u}\psi|+|\partial_{v}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\psi|.

(B.10)

Proof.

Recalling (
B.2
), (
B.3
), note first that

*d\big(\psi^{2}\varpi_{2}\big)=-\delta_{1}\left(\frac{\psi^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{2}{r}\psi\partial_{r}\psi\right)dt-\delta_{1}\frac{2}{r^{2}}\psi\partial_{t}\psi\ x^{i}dx^{i}=\delta_{1}\left(-\frac{\psi^{2}}{2r^{2}}+\frac{2}{r}\psi\partial_{u}\psi\right)du-\delta_{1}\left(\frac{\psi^{2}}{2r^{2}}+\frac{2}{r}\psi\partial_{v}\psi\right)dv.

Recall the expressions (
B.4
) for the normals to the hypersurfaces
\Sigma_{\tau}
and
\underline{C}_{v}
. The fluxes corresponding to
J^{V_{i},w_{i},q_{i},\varpi_{i}}[\psi]
satisfy

J^{V_{1},w_{1},q_{1},\varpi_{1}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}=(\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2},\qquad J^{V_{1},w_{1},q_{1},\varpi_{1}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}=(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2},

\displaystyle J^{V_{2},w_{2},q_{2},\varpi_{2}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{1}}{r^{2}}(\partial_{v}(r\psi))^{2}+\frac{\delta_{1}}{4r^{2}}|\not{\nabla}(r\psi)|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{1}}{r^{2}}\iota_{r\leq R}(\partial_{u}(r\psi))^{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}J^{V_{1},w_{1},q_{1},\varpi_{1}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}+\delta_{1}\left(\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\psi^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\psi\partial_{v}\psi+\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\psi^{2}\iota_{r\leq R}-\frac{1}{r}\psi\partial_{u}\psi\iota_{r\leq R}\right),

\displaystyle J^{V_{2},w_{2},q_{2},\varpi_{2}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{1}}{4r^{2}}(\partial_{u}(r\psi))^{2}+\frac{\delta_{1}}{8r^{2}}|\not{\nabla}(r\psi)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}J^{V_{1},w_{1},q_{1},\varpi_{1}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}+\delta_{1}\left(\frac{1}{4r^{2}}\psi^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\psi\partial_{u}\psi\right),

\displaystyle|J^{V_{3},w_{3},q_{3},\varpi_{3}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{2}C((\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(1+r)^{-2}|\psi|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}),

\displaystyle|J^{V_{3},w_{3},q_{3},\varpi_{3}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{2}C((\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(1+r)^{-2}|\psi|^{2}),

and the bulk terms satisfy

K^{V_{1},w_{1},q_{1}}[\psi]=0,\qquad H^{V_{1},w_{1}}[\psi]=(\partial_{u}\psi+\partial_{v}\psi),

K^{V_{2},w_{2},q_{2}}[\psi]=0,\qquad H^{V_{2},w_{2}}[\psi]=\delta_{1}(\partial_{u}\psi+\partial_{v}\psi),

\displaystyle K^{V_{3},w_{3},q_{3}}[\psi]=\

\displaystyle\delta_{2}\left(\frac{1}{r}-\delta_{3}\Big(\frac{1}{r(1+r)^{\delta}}+\frac{\delta}{2(1+r)^{1+\delta}}\Big)\right)|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta_{2}\delta_{3}\delta}{2(1+r)^{1+\delta}}\left((\partial_{t}\psi)^{2}+(\partial_{r}\psi)^{2}\right)+\frac{\delta_{2}\delta_{3}\delta(1+\delta)}{2r(1+r)^{2+\delta}}\psi^{2},

\displaystyle H^{V_{3},w_{3}}[\psi]=\

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{3}}{(1+r)^{\delta}}\right)(\partial_{v}\psi-\partial_{u}\psi)+\frac{\delta_{2}}{r}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{3}}{(1+r)^{\delta}}\right)\psi.

It thus follows that the coercivity relations (
B.7
)–(
B.9
), along with the property (
B.10
), hold if
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
and
\delta_{3}
are chosen according to (
B.5
).
∎

B.2
The
J_{\rm far}
current

The quadruples
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{{V}}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{{w}}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{{q}}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{{\varpi}}^{\circ}_{\rm far})
and
(\tilde{V}_{\rm far},\tilde{w}_{\rm far},\tilde{q}_{\rm far},\tilde{\varpi}_{\rm far})
of Section
3.5
are defined as follows. Consider some

0<\delta_{6}\ll\delta_{5}\ll\delta_{4}\ll\delta_{3}\ll\delta_{2}\ll\delta_{1}\ll 1.

(B.11)

Noting that
-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x^{\mu}}udx^{\mu}=-\frac{1}{2}du=(\partial_{v})^{\flat}
, define

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far}=r^{p}\partial_{v},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far}=\frac{r^{p-1}}{2},\qquad(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far})_{\mu}=-\frac{\partial_{x^{\mu}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}{2}-\Big(\frac{p}{4}-\frac{\delta_{4}}{2}\Big)r^{p-2}\partial_{x^{\mu}}u,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}=\Big(-\frac{r^{p-1}}{4}+2\delta_{5}r^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\Big)r^{2}\sin\vartheta d\vartheta\wedge d\varphi.

Define then

\tilde{V}_{\rm far}=\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}V,\qquad\tilde{w}_{\rm far}=\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}w,\qquad\tilde{q}_{\rm far}=\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}q,\qquad\tilde{\varpi}_{\rm far}=\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}\varpi,

where
V
,
w
,
q
,
\varpi
are defined by (
B.6
), so that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}_{\rm far}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far}+\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}V,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}_{\rm far}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far}+\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}w,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}_{\rm far}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far}+\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}q,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}_{\rm far}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}+\frac{1}{\delta_{6}}\varpi.

Proposition B.2.1

(The
J_{\rm far}
current in Minkowski space)
.

With
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}_{\rm far})
defined as above, if
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{6}
are chosen according to (
B.11
), the associated currents
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\rm far}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\rm far}
, defined by (
3.11
), (
3.12
) satisfy the weighted bulk coercivity properties (
3.40
) and the weighted boundary coercivity property (
3.41
). More precisely, for
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
and
r\geq R
,

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\rm far}{}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{p-2}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}(\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2},

(B.12)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\rm far}{}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\gtrsim(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}+r^{p}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-2}\psi^{2},

(B.13)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\rm far}[\psi]

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{p-3}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+r^{p-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{-1-\delta}|\partial_{u}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-3}\psi^{2}.

(B.14)

Moreover the corresponding
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\rm far}[\psi]
satisfies

|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\rm far}[\psi]|\lesssim r^{p-1}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|+|\partial_{u}\psi|+|\partial_{v}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\psi|.

(B.15)

Proof.

Recall again (
B.3
) and note that

\displaystyle*\,d(\psi^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far})=\

\displaystyle\left(\Big(\frac{p+1}{8}r^{p-2}-\delta_{5}\frac{p+2}{2}r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\Big)\psi^{2}-\Big(\frac{r^{p-1}}{2}-4\delta_{5}r^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\Big)\psi\partial_{u}\psi\right)du

\displaystyle+\left(\Big(\frac{p+1}{8}r^{p-2}-\delta_{5}\frac{p+2}{2}r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\Big)\psi^{2}+\Big(\frac{r^{p-1}}{2}-4\delta_{5}r^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\Big)\psi\partial_{v}\psi\right)dv.

Note moreover that

(\pi^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far}})^{\sharp\sharp}=pr^{p-1}\partial_{v}\otimes\partial_{v}-\frac{pr^{p-1}}{2}\big(\partial_{u}\otimes\partial_{v}+\partial_{v}\otimes\partial_{u}\big)+\frac{r^{p-3}}{2}\big(\partial_{\vartheta}\otimes\partial_{\vartheta}+\sin^{-2}\vartheta\partial_{\varphi}\otimes\partial_{\varphi}\big),

and

\nabla^{\mu}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far})_{\mu}=\frac{pr^{p-3}}{4}-\frac{\delta_{4}pr^{p-3}}{2}.

The fluxes corresponding to
J^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}[\psi]
satisfy, for
r\geq R
,

\displaystyle J^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=r^{p-2}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}-\delta_{5}\left(\frac{p+2}{2}r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2}+4r^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\psi\partial_{v}\psi\right),

(B.16)

\displaystyle=r^{p-2}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+\delta_{5}\left(\frac{(2-p)}{2}r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2}-4r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi\partial_{v}(r\psi)\right),

\displaystyle J^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{p}}{4}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{4}}{2}r^{p-2}\psi^{2}-\delta_{5}\left(\frac{p+2}{2}r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2}-4r^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\psi\partial_{u}\psi\right),

(B.17)

and the bulk terms satisfy

\displaystyle K^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}[\psi]=\

\displaystyle pr^{p-3}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+\frac{(2-p)r^{p-1}}{4}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{4}(2-p)}{2}r^{p-3}\psi^{2}-2\delta_{4}r^{p-3}\psi\partial_{v}(r\psi)

(B.18)

\displaystyle H^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}[\psi]=\

\displaystyle r^{p-1}\partial_{v}(r\psi).

(B.19)

In particular, if
\delta_{4}
and
\delta_{5}
are chosen according to (
B.11
) (depending on
p
), then

\displaystyle J^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{p-2}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}(\partial_{v}\psi)^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\psi^{2},

(B.20)

\displaystyle J^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\varpi}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}_{\mu}[\psi]n_{\underline{C}_{v}}^{\mu}+(\partial_{u}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{p}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+r^{p-2}\psi^{2},

(B.21)

and

\displaystyle K^{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{V}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{w}^{\circ}_{\rm far},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{q}^{\circ}_{\rm far}}[\psi]

\displaystyle\gtrsim pr^{p-3}|\partial_{v}(r\psi)|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-1}|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+p(2-p)r^{p-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}+(2-p)r^{p-3}\psi^{2}.

(B.22)

It follows from Proposition
B.1.1
that the currents
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{\rm far}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{\rm far}
satisfy the weighted bulk coercivity properties (
B.12
)–(
B.14
) provided
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{6}
are chosen according to (
B.11
), and that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{\rm far}
satisfies (
B.15
).
∎

The inequalities (
B.10
) and (
B.15
) in particular mean that the
r^{-1}|\not{\nabla}\psi|
term on the left hand side of the assumed inequality (
4.30
) is superfluous in the case that
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
is Minkowski space. This term is estimated in the proof of Proposition
4.7.2
nonetheless in order to illustrate how it is estimated in the case of Kerr.

Appendix C
Verifying the null condition assumption

In this section the proof of Proposition
4.7.2
is given. Proposition
4.7.2
can be more precisely stated as follows.

Proposition C.0.1
.

Assumption
4.7.1
holds for the classical null condition of Klainerman
[
Kla86
]
on Minkowski space and more generally the class of equations on Kerr considered in Luk
[
Luk13
]
.

More precisely,
if
(\mathcal{M},g_{0})
is either Minkowski space or a member of the Kerr family and if

F=N^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\partial_{\nu}\psi

satisfies the assumption (
C.22
), then there exists
k_{\rm null}>0
, and for all
k\geq k_{\rm null}
, there exists

\varepsilon_{\rm null}>0
, such that we have the following.

Let
\psi
be a smooth function in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)

satisfying (
4.29
) for

0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm null}
.
Then for all
\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
,

\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau^{\prime},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}\Big(|\underline{L}\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+|L\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|\Big)\left|\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\right|\\
+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau^{\prime},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F)^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),

and moreover,

\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau^{\prime},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}\Big(r^{p-1}|L(r\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi)|+|\underline{L}\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+|L\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\not{\nabla}\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|+\frac{1}{r}|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\psi|\Big)\left|\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F\right|\\
+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau^{\prime},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}(\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}F)^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

The estimate of Proposition
C.0.1
is only nontrivial for large
r
